1. The Blacksmith and the Witch

Author's Note:

Hi all! I'm excited to share this with anyone who is willing to read it and I hope you enjoy! Chapters will each be posted with their own trigger warnings/tags since not every chapter will contain violent/explicit imagery. Chapters will also range anywhere from 7-15K depending on the length of the section that i break it into since i just started writing this for fun and didn't think about chapter breaks very much lol.

This world was strange, certainly, but Kara loved it. From the time she was old enough to crawl, Kara was entranced by the Expanse. The Expanse was a vast ocean that covered most of the world with violent, ripping waves formed by indescribably powerful winds that made travel across the water impossible. As far as anyone knew, there was only one mass of land on the whole planet. The whole of the land was the kingdom Omnis, ruled by the Luthor family.

Kara lived with her mother on a farm about a mile away from the nearest town and often sat on the porch facing the Expanse to daydream. She imagined herself as a brave, strong knight who could save any princess from any sort of peril. 

On this day, Kara and her mother stood at the precipice of a huge marble cliff, overlooking a thin strip of beachy sand below. The wind whipped Kara’s golden hair around her face, and threatened to knock her little legs out from under her. 

“ Jeju ,” Kara asked as she tugged on the sleeve of her mother’s dress, “Why can’t we play in the water?”

“Oh, little one,” she ran her fingers gently through Kara’s golden hair, “the water is beautiful, yes, deep blue like your eyes.” 

She smiled down at her daughter, so small and precious clinging to her skirt trying to hide from the wind. A sad smile fell across her face. 

“But it is also very dangerous.”

Kara’s face fell into a tiny frown.

“You must promise me you’ll stay away from the water,” she demanded.

“I promise, Jeju,” Kara’s small voice rang out.

Her mother nodded and took Kara’s hand as she led them back to the village at the bottom of the steep hill. They were in town for supplies for their farm that day. 

Many years later, when Kara had just turned thirteen, her mother took her back to the precipice, promising to give her a good reason to stay away from the water. Together they sat looking over the water, sitting in a slightly tense silence as the wind whipped around them. 

Her mother took a deep breath and fixed her tired eyes on the horizon.

“When your father and I first met, we would play in the waters under this cliff every day. We fell in love here,” she smiled sadly, “After you were born, my love for the water wavered out of fear for your safety. I couldn’t imagine you being hurt by something I loved.”

She paused for a moment, long enough that Kara almost thought she’d forgotten to finish her story. Just as she was about to ask what happened next, her mother took a shuddering breath and continued. 

“But your father… your father still loved the Expanse. He said he could feel its raw power and mystery when he touched it. He loved to wade out into the water, and said the dangerous creatures were too big to come into such shallow water. He said that it was only the small beautiful creatures with him.”

Though she was still young, Kara could read the tone of the story, and began to feel scared.

“He was right… partially. Do you see the beach below us, Kara?”

“Yes, Jeju.”

“Just past the beach, the water is very shallow. I could walk out maybe fifty paces, and only then would the water reach my waist. But only ten paces past that, the shore stops. It just drops off into the open water. Nobody knows how deep it is, but we do know that there are things far greater than us in that water.”

Kara gulped.

“Your father went out his usual fifty paces while I sat on the beach. You were perhaps a year old at the time, playing in the sand, having a grand time, really. But I think he was enraptured by the Expanse. I noticed he was going out further than he usually did, but I paid no mind. I… I thought that he would be more mindful. I should have called him back.”

Her mother was crying now, tears but no sobs, and Kara felt her own eyes prickling in response.

“I only looked down for a moment; you’d put sand in your mouth and I was trying to get it out. I looked back and he was just… gone. I picked you up and I couldn’t find him anywhere. I called out for him, but he was just gone. And then the water. The-” she swallowed, “The color wasn’t right where he’d been. And then this thing, I didn’t see all of it, but I saw the tentacles… and the teeth.”

Noting her daughter’s abject horror as she gaped at her mother, she cleared her throat and continued with less gory detail. 

“I never went back in the water after that. It is true, creatures like the one I saw that day are too big to swim up to the shelf, so it should be safe. But, if you get too close to the edge, it’s anyone’s guess as to what horrible thing can snatch you.”

Kara was crying in earnest now, tears streaming down her face as she stared in horror at the water below. Her mother gathered her into a crushing hug and they both cried: her mother for the loss of a loved one, and Kara out of a deep, primal fear that rooted itself in her chest, a fear not for the Expanse, but what it contained as she swore to herself that she could see all the giant creatures writhing in the water just off the shore.

About fifty miles away, in a castle made of heavy black stone, a young girl was thrown into a cell in the dungeon, beaten and bloody. The door slammed shut behind her and her green eyes glowed in the darkness of the room as she felt hot blood trickle down her back. She clenched her fists and felt a massive amount of power start to flow from her fingertips. The wrought iron hinges of the door bent and crumpled like paper with a horrible screeching noise, and the heavy wooden door fell to the floor with a terrible clatter. 

 

-------------

 

Kara looked up at the night sky, her head propped on her rucksack. She’d doused her fire moments before, so the residual heat from the coals warmed her, but the absence of flames gave her a much better view. 

On rare nights like this, the sky was clear, the wind was still, and all the small forest creatures were only a dull noise in the trees. On nights like this, the sky was beautiful; on nights like this, Kara could see the four moons all in a row surrounded by a dazzling array of stars. It was simply breathtaking. 

Kara loved the sky. More specifically, she loved the place beyond the sky where there were no clouds, there were only moons, and stars, and, on the even rarer occasion, Kara could see her favorite constellation: The Witch’s Face. It had the comically pointed nose and seemed to have a pointed hat to match if Kara imagined hard enough. Her constellation was a summer one, meaning that it came over the horizon during the worst weather of the year when the clouds rolled in from the Expanse and heavily blanketed the entire sky with thick rain for nearly the entire season. She closed her eyes and tried to get lost in the warmth radiating from the coals of her dying campfire.    

She and her mother had loved these nights, and since her mother’s untimely death ten years prior, they had been painfully bittersweet. Not a month after Kara’s mother told her about her father’s death, Maxwell Lord, under the order of Queen Lillian Luthor, had slaughtered Kara’s entire village and burned her farm to the ground. All of this because of an unfounded rumor that the town was going to revolt. Kara had only survived because she wasn’t in the town, or at her farm when it happened. She tried not to think too much about that fact.

While she sat on the cliff overlooking the Expanse, one-hundred armed guards raided the tiny sleepy village of Krypton and her mother’s farm. By the time Kara noticed the smoke from the fires, it was far too late. Her entire town was gone leaving her all alone in her world. So much misfortune had fallen upon her in her mere thirteen years of life. 

Her luck had only slightly picked up when Jeremiah Danvers, a leader in the secret resistance against the Luthors, found her sitting in the center of her destroyed village five days after the attack. Her golden hair was dirty and matted and her eyes looked unfocused. She was clinging to the only worldly possession she’d had left: a crimson blanket her mother had woven for her when she was a child. He’d taken her back to his home in Strongfair, and he and his wife nursed her back to health. Their daughter, Alex, was the one to coax Kara out of her shell and get her to tell her story. 

Jeremiah had been furious at the news, knowing it had to have been the work of the Luthors. He and a secret congregation of rebels organized an attack on one of the smaller villages outside the heart of the kingdom called Willowdale, where much of the royal army’s weaponry was made.  

Things went sideways quickly. A spy in the resistance had reported everything back to the royals, and the attack was met with an entire royal battalion that quickly swept through the smaller force. 

Jeremiah did not survive,

Weeks were spent mourning: mourning Jeremiah, mourning the loss of the one hope against the Luthors, mourning the brokenness of a family. 

Alex and Kara depended upon one another in the months following; for security, for comfort, and for the love of a family that had been ripped away from both of them. 

And now Kara and Alex were both grown: Alex a spy for the resistance placed in the royal army, and Kara a blacksmith. Of course, Kara would not willingly help the tyrants that ruled over the kingdom; she only made weapons for Alex, whom she considered her sister, and for the resistance which had been building in number since the devastating retaliation that had taken Alex’s father. 

Now, as she lay under the innumerable stars in the sky, she was on a new mission, a mission solely to destroy Maxwell Lord. 

He’d been stealing weapons from her for months to supply himself in battle, a crime punishable by the dismemberment of the hands, but she couldn’t prove it. He was always so sneaky and slimy and Kara could never pin him as the perpetrator. That was why she was here: three miles away from the one witch in the land who had no qualms about ruining the lives of the royals. The Nightshade Witch. 

Kara had heard rumors of the witch in town after a particularly nasty curse had been cast upon a man who lived in Willowdale, where Kara now resided. He’d been caught sleeping with a number of married women whose husbands decided it was time to get even. The details of the curse were never made clear to her, but Kara did hear that he would not bed another woman in his lifetime. 

She needed the witch to avenge her parents and Jeremiah. No mortal could accomplish the feat on their own; the royals were too heavily guarded for any one person to destroy them. As strong as Kara was, she needed help, which is how she ended up in the middle of the Deathwoods all by herself. One of the older townspeople had told her how to get to the witch’s house, but he’d failed to mention that it was a two day’s journey from where she lived in Willowdale. 

Kara sighed.

“I should be there no later than sunfall tomorrow,” she tried to encourage herself. A swig of her strongest booze, a spicy mix called Glowing Tempest, and an equal gulp of water from her pack had Kara ready to settle down for the night, and she slipped into a peaceful sleep.

Peaceful for several hours until she was rudely awakened, that is.

As Kara slept, the sun began to peak over the tops of the trees, and an enchanted patch of Firethorn began creeping toward her. She didn’t wake up until it had slithered its way around her right arm from wrist to shoulder and its venom began seeping through her sleeve. 

“Aagh!” Kara jerked awake and scrambled to pull her arm away. This only made the plant wrap around her arm tighter and the thorns bit into her skin like teeth while the venom began to sear the flesh beneath her shirt. Kara’s free hand scrambled for her dagger, and the sharp blade made quick work of the plant. 

She rolled up her sleeve and cursed as she saw the damage done by the Firethorn. A perfect blistered spiral wound all the way up her arm, and the pain was intense. 

“Rao help me,” she muttered as she ripped the sleeve from her shirt, wet it with the last of the water from her canteen, and wrapped the worst part of the burn near the top of her bicep. 

“I hope this witch is friendlier than people make her out to be. Maybe she’ll at least be kind enough to give me some water,” she muttered.

She packed up her bag quickly, eager to set out and avoid more of the aggressive plantlife. She trekked on for what felt like an eternity as her mouth became unbearably dry and her wounds chafed against her shirt with every movement. However, she did not become discouraged as the undergrowth began to thicken, and the rate at which she encountered traps set in the form of enchanted plants grew increasingly frequent, meaning she was getting close. The Nightshade Witch apparently was a sort of jack-of-all-trades kind of sorceress, but she specialized in elemental magic, or so Kara had been told. 

After a few miles, Kara was exhausted. She’d used the last of her water that morning and it had been hours since she had had anything to eat. Just as Kara was considering turning back to find a source of water, she stumbled through the trees and undergrowth into a small clearing no longer than one-hundred paces across. In the middle sat a squat, greystone hut with a wooden roof covered in straw and a dark oak door. 

“Finally,” Kara murmured as she stumbled closer to the door. Her pack weighed heavily on her shoulder, as did the sword that hung from her belt in its scabbard, and the knife strapped to the back of her belt. She knocked feebly three times and stood back on shaky legs for an answer. Kara’s vision began to grow blurry from dehydration in combination with the Firethorn venom, and when the door finally opened and a dark blurry figure became visible, all she could muster was a weak “Hi, I’m Kara, and I think I’m dying,” before she collapsed onto the cobblestone outside. 

 

When Kara woke up she was in a dark room lit dimly by three candles at the head of the cot she was lying on. As she let her vision adjust she tried to get her bearings; she felt the blankets on the bed: an unfamiliar fur that was silky and soft to the touch, a sturdy wood bed frame with intricate designs that Kara couldn’t quite see in the dim light, and piece of cold metal attached to a sort of lacey material that, as soon as she touched it, jerked away from her hand.

“You can’t just collapse on my doorstep and then feel me up,” a smooth, steely voice said from the shadows.

“Huh? Where am… I can’t see anything. Who are you and where am I?” Kara asked as she sat up, wincing when her burned arm touched the fabric of her shirt. 

“You’re in my house, in my bed where I so kindly put you after you nearly fell on top of me, so I should be the one asking the questions. Who are you and why are you here?” The voice demanded. 

“I- I’m from Willowdale and I still can’t see,” she replied haughtily as she swung her feet over the edge of the bed onto the cool stone floor. 

The body that Kara could vaguely attach to the voice in the dark sighed irritatedly and snapped its fingers. Suddenly, dozens more candles around the room were lit and the black curtains over a small window on the far wall from Kara were drawn back. Blinking her eyes into the new light, Kara got a good look at the person who was with her, and her heart promptly skipped a beat. 

The woman before her wore a long, tightly-fitted, black gown decorated with intricate lace patterns up the bodice, Darkwing feathers adorning the shoulders, giving the illusion that the wearer could possibly fly if she wanted to, and a flat, bronze skull set in the center over her sternum. Kara could have picked numerous more details out of the dress but the woman wearing it was simply too beautiful for her to tear her eyes away. 

So pale she could be a ghost, her startling emerald eyes were set beautifully under defined brows and above high cheekbones. Her sooty makeup seemed to drip like black, godly ichor from her eyes with tears of gold shimmering down the paths the black marked out. Kara was pretty sure the line of her jaw was sharper than any sword she had ever forged, and she couldn’t help but let her eyes trace it from one side of her face, down to the center of her chin, to the other side where her gaze wandered up to her ear, only partially hidden by raven black hair, and Kara could see numerous ruby-red piercings set in the cartilage there. 

“ Oh Hell’s fury she’s so pretty, ” Kara thought to herself as her mouth hung slightly agape. Her brain eventually realized that the metal and lace she had felt had been the chest piece of her dress and that she had definitely laid a heavy hand across her.  

“Shit.”

She was only shaken from her reverie when the woman waved her hand in front of Kara’s face with an exasperated expression.

“Hello? Anyone there?” 

“Oh, I- uh,” Kara’s jaw snapped shut as she tried to appear as though she hadn’t just had an out-of-body experience. 

“Before you so kindly collapsed in my doorway, I think you said your name is Kara. Is that right?” the woman asked with a perfect raised eyebrow. 

“I- yes that’s me, Kara Danvers” Kara could feel her cheeks burning with embarrassment.

“Well Kara Danvers , first of all you have drool on your face, and second, tell me why you are here before I start becoming inhospitable,” she leveled her intense emerald stare at Kara’s own ocean blue one. 

Kara aggressively wiped the drool from her chin, embarrassed.

“I- uh, I actually needed help from the Nightshade Witch. Do you know where I could find her?”

“You’re looking at her,” the corner of the woman’s mouth quirked up in a smirk.

“Oh!” Kara exclaimed, “perfect!” 

“Hold on,” the woman held up her hand, “What have you brought me in return for my services? My spells command a hefty price, if I’m even willing to help,” her voice felt like stones smoothed by a river in Kara’s ears: so hard, but soft if she felt it the right way.

Kara tried to ignore the way she said “services” dropped directly to a place below her gut and scrambled for an answer. 

“I can do anything you need. Within reason that is,” she added as she saw a pleasantly surprised gleam flash over the witch’s eyes. 

“And just what do you deem ‘within reason,’ Miss Danvers?” she seemed to purr her name and a light shiver ran up Kara’s spine that she hoped the woman didn’t notice. 

“If you need any work done on your house, or if you are in need of weapons, or if you need manual labor of sorts, I can provide that,” Kara answered, hoping the woman didn’t notice the effect that she had on her. 

“Hmmm… tempting. I can think of a few things that are in dire need of repair here, but I’ll wait to assign you to anything until I hear what you need,” She snapped her fingers and a chair flew in from behind a curtain blocking the room off from the rest of the house. It flew in so fast Kara was afraid it would sweep the woman’s legs out from under her, but it stopped abruptly, just short of her calves, and she sat down in a graceful motion. 

Kara was staring again, amazed.

“Well? Tell me what you want,” the woman’s voice was pleasant, certainly, but had a bite to it that made Kara feel that she should tread lightly with her words. 

“There’s a man in town, Maxwell Lord,” the woman gave a disgusted grunt of recognition,

“I refuse to sell my weapons to him, so he steals them, which is a highly punishable offense, as I’m sure you know,” the woman nodded, “But I can neither catch him in the act, nor prove that he is stealing. Thousands of shillings of my work has been taken to support an army I despise and I cannot fix it on my own, which is why I need your help.”     

The woman thought for a moment.

“So… you despise the royal army?” she asked.

“They slaughtered my birth mother and my adoptive father, so yes I do.”

“I see…” she cradled her chin on one hand as she leaned forward and rested her elbow on her knee. She studied Kara as if seeing her in a new light. 

Kara sat on the edge of the bed, fidgeting nervously.

“Very well. I’ll help you.”

“You will??” Kara asked, surprised. 

“Don’t sound so shocked,” she huffed, irritated.

“No! I just- I’m sorry,” Kara bowed her head, “it’s just that people in town made it seem like you don’t normally help people so easily…”

“Well, normally not, but since Maxwell Lord is more or less a right-hand-man to Queen Lillian, this is a perfect opportunity to get under Mommy Dearest’s skin,” she sat back as a devious smile crept across her face.

“What do you mean ‘Mommy Dearest?’”

“My mother is Queen Lillian. I’m sure you’ve heard rumors of her daughter who ‘went missing’ about ten years ago,” she said.

“Wait a second. You mean you’re Princess Lena? I thought the royals reported that you’d died all those years ago!” Kara exclaimed, shock obvious on her face.

“Well, I did die, at least in Mother’s eyes. She gave her soldiers the go-ahead to completely needlessly massacre the village of Krypton. I vehemently disagreed and tried my best to stop it from happening, but I was only fourteen. Not one of those awful soldiers would listen to me, and two of them, including Maxwell Lord, dragged me back to the palace where I was beaten and locked away like a shame they could hide. So I left,” she stated simply, no emotion to her voice. 

The knowledge that Lena wanted to save her village gave Kara a warm feeling in her chest that she didn’t want to think about too much.

“I- Lady Luthor, I’m so sorry to hear that, I-” she was cut off.

“No offense, but I don’t need your sympathy, Miss Danvers. I’ve made my peace with it. And please, call me Lena.”

“Yes Miss- of course Lena. But if I’m calling you by your first name, you should use mine as well,” she said with a small smile.

“Very well, Kara,” the tiniest of smiles was returned.

Both women seemed to be seeing each other clearly for the first time; Lena’s eyes roamed Kara’s face to her injured arm, where her expression turned a bit sour. 

“What happened to your arm?”

“I fell asleep too near to a patch of Firethorn, apparently,” Kara chuckled as she turned the majority of the burns away from Lena’s view. 

“Oh so you're the one that maimed my plants on the east rim,” she thought for a moment, “I can make a salve to expedite the healing if you’d be willing to do a little chore for me?”

“Of course! Name your price,” Kara said with a smile and an impulsive wink, and, if the lighting were better, she may have been able to see the light rosy blush that spread across Lena’s cheeks. 

“You,” she cleared her throat, “you can fix the roof for me. The protection spell I put on it is due to wear out soon, and I can feel a storm in the air.”

“How can you feel a storm? Do you use some kind of spell? The sky is so clear right now.” 

Lena chuckled, “No spell necessary. I’ve lived out here by myself since I ran away, so about ten years I think. Nature becomes predictable when you’ve been living in it long enough.”

“Okay but how can you tell; you only told me why ,” Kara insisted with a smile that Lena hated to think of as adorable . 

“Very well. Close your eyes.”

Kara gave her a strange look but obeyed anyway.

“Now what?”

“First of all, be patient,” she smiled, “and second, listen: all the animals outside have stopped chattering. They’re all hiding from the storm.”

Kara’s brows knitted together as she tried her best to listen to the world around them. 

“Hey you’re right!” she exclaimed.

“Did you assume I would be wrong?” Lena asked teasingly.

“No! I just- I never noticed it before. I used to live right at the edge of the Expanse where the worst storms happen. I just can’t believe I never noticed it,” Kara said shyly, eyes still closed.

“No worries, I was just teasing you Kara,” she smiled and was thankful Kara wasn’t looking at her because she was definitely staring.

“What else tips you off? If you don’t notice the quiet, I mean.”

“Feel the temperature in the air; it’s dropped slightly since this conversation began. I’d say we have maybe fifteen to twenty minutes until the storm clouds are over us.”

“Amazing,” Kara whispered, her eyes still closed. 

Lena caught herself staring yet again and cleared her throat.

“Uh-Yes, it’s pretty handy. But if you don’t want to fix the roof in the rain you should probably get a move on,” she said looking away as Kara reopened her piercing blue eyes.

“Of course!” Kara gave her best million-shilling smile and stood up from the bed, only to hit her head on a low-hanging bundle of spices which earned a hearty laugh from Lena. As Kara rose to her full height, Lena finally realized just how imposing Kara’s presence was. She had to have been at least a head and a half taller than Lena, and completely muscle bound. 

Kara grumbled something about ‘huts built only for dwarves nowadays,’ and stepped more gracefully around Lena. As she passed, Lena caught a whiff of Kara’s scent: all earthy with noticeable hints of pine. 

“What does she wear to smell like that? Does she just smell like that naturally? No way does anyone who slept in the woods and passed out on the floor smell that good. How tall is she? Six feet? Taller? And she’s so… strong looking,” Lena rattled off in her mind all the frantic thoughts that Kara caused her while ignoring the warmth building in her chest.

She followed Kara out to the front, and her estimations were correct; she could just see some storm clouds starting to roll over the trees at the edge of the clearing, the wind was steadily building, and the temperature was dropping quickly. 

Lena was just about to offer Kara a ladder to aid her ascent onto the roof, but Kara, in a very impressive feat of strength, just grabbed onto the edge of the lowest part of the house’s walls and lifted herself up with only a small grunt of effort. Lena’s panic only increased at the display and her heart gave a hefty ba-bump as she watched Kara’s arm flex in the absence of her sleeve. 

“ Thank the gods for that Firethorn,” is what she thought, but what she said was, “How do you plan on fixing the roof when you didn’t bring any tools up with you?”

Kara reached into a pouch hanging from the belt of her pants and pulled out a light brown powder.

“Sawdust,” she said, “When the rain washes it down into the cracks, it’ll expand and stop the leaks. It pretty much seals itself up.”

“I see. I would have had you do a bit more work for me had I known you would finish this task so easily,” Lena teased with a smirk.

“Please no,” Kara said with a small grimace, “I already felt some of the blisters on my arm pop when I got up here. It doesn’t really feel all that good.” She quickly finished packing saw dust into the spots on the roof where the cracks were more evident and the straw was thinner. 

“Well then, while you do that I’ll see if I can’t do anything to make you feel good again,” Lena replied, not thinking at all about the implications of her words until she heard a startled yelp and a whump , and she turned around to find that Kara had slipped off the roof and fallen into one of the bushes. The sight of her boot-clad feet sticking out of the plant while Kara tried and failed to get up was humorous enough that, by the time she was done laughing, the golden tears of her makeup weren’t the only ones rolling down Lena’s face.

“You know,” Kara grunted as she tried and failed to pull herself out of the bush, “I could use a little help if you aren’t too busy. Glad to give you a laugh.”

Lena stifled her laughter with one hand while she offered the other to Kara. Kara’s hand was rough and calloused from her years in the forge, but also soft in a way that Lena couldn’t quite explain. After she was righted, she gave an indignant huff and tried to pull the leaves that were stuck in her hair out. Lena took pity on her and removed the bits that were harder to reach. Kara was so tall Lena had to stand on her tiptoes and steady herself with a hand on Kara’s chest to reach. She didn’t notice how close she was until she saw how stiff Kara was and how hard she was obviously trying to not make eye contact. 

Lena stepped back half a pace and cleared her throat.

“Are you okay, Kara?”

“Uh- yeah. Yeah, yeah, yeah, I’m great, super even!” Kara rambled with an embarrassed smile and a thumbs up. She looked over her shoulder.

“I’m sorry about the bush, though.”

“Oh, don’t worry about that. Nothing I can’t fix.”

Even though Kara knew that she could use magic, she was still in awe when, with a wave of her hand and an incantation muttered under her breath, Lena mended the bush, and Kara couldn’t tell that it had been damaged at all. 

“That is… so freaking cool,” Kara said, wide-eyed.

“Oh that? That’s nothing. You should see what I can do with a real spell,” Lena smiled cockily back at her. Maybe she should have just accepted the compliment though, because the awed expression Kara was giving her was making her grow rather hot under the collar of her dress. She thanked all the gods that be when the first fat drop of rain fell onto Kara’s nose so she could use it as an excuse to turn away from Kara and duck back inside. 

Kara followed her inside and removed her worn down leather shoes and placed them neatly by the front door.

2. The Stars and The Stories

Summary for the Chapter:

An unexpected visitor changes Lena's and Kara's plans.

Notes for the Chapter:

Trigger warnings for blood and graphic depictions of violence this chapter, as well as minor abuse mention. But there's also some fluff so it balances out a little bit.

The next morning when Lena awoke, the air of the cottage was crisp and cool, and smelled clean and dewey in the early light. The air around her was cold, but the bed and the blankets were warm and comforting, and she couldn’t resist burying her nose back into the blankets that smelled so much like Kara. 

Kara.

Lena didn’t know why she’d had so much of an affect on her right from the beginning, and she smiled to herself picturing her cute drunken face the night before. Lena sat up and looked around; Kara was not in the bed nor the bedroom. She noticed Kara’s one sleeved tunic hanging over the bedroom door and slipped it over her shoulders (it was cold after all). “None taken, I think it’s a noble pursuit.” 

“Only problem is that the queen has an army of a hundred thousand strong and a dragon on her side. I didn’t even think dragons were really real until I saw it flying across the sky over Krypton the day before it was destroyed.” 

“Yes, Sanguis is a problem for the resistance. I once asked my mother where she found him and all she told me was that there was a place far away that no one could reach where dragons rule the land.” 

“Far away? But the only land on the planet is here. Even the most experienced sailors from the villages have never found anything past our shores.” 

“Not necessarily. My theory is that there is another mass of land across the world opposite from us whose wildlife has been able to flourish and evolve because we can’t get there and ruin the environment. Mother must have had another witch make a spell so she could cross the Expanse.” 

“If only we could get our hands on a dragon or two to even the odds; we might actually be able to win this war.” 

”Don't think that I haven’t considered it, but my magic isn’t strong enough for a teleportation spell to a place as far away as that. even just moving all of my body matter from one place to another place less than ten feet away is excruciatingly draining. And besides I have to know where it is that I’m going to execute it.”

“Too bad we can’t just go there on a boat. The waters are way too dangerous, and there’s so many… things in the water.” Kara visibly shivered. 

“Things? What do you mean?” 

Kara took a deep breath, “My mother used to tell me how she and my father would swim in the water near the shores of the Expanse. They thought it was safe because only the small creatures came that close to the shore. My father… was dragged from the shallows by a huge monstrous thing with tentacles and teeth and-“ Kara’s voice broke a little and Lena reached out a comforting hand to her shoulder. 

“I’m sorry Kara.” 

“It’s okay, I was just a baby when it happened. I just remember my mother’s face when she told me that story.” Kara closed her eyes and tried to shake the vision of pain away. 

Lena’s thumb stroked gently over Kara’s skin and before either one of them knew what was happening, they were both wrapped deep into a hug that neither one knew who initiated. Kara’s strong arms enveloped Lena and she’d never felt more safe in her life. Kara was so tall that Lena’s head pressed into the center of her chest, not quite reaching her shoulder.  Lena was supposed to be the one comforting Kara, but Lena was getting just as much out of the hug as Kara was. 

Lena, standing on her toes, buried her face into Kara’s neck and whispered, “I’m sorry, Kara.” 

Her arms wrapped tighter around Lena and Kara nuzzled her face against the top of Lena’s head and inhaled deeply, taking in her comforting scent. Lena’s dark curls fell around her face and sheltered her from the outside world against Kara’s chest. Kara had never felt a sense of peace comparable to this before. 

Neither one of them knew how long they had been standing there locked in each other’s arms, but neither seemed to want to break the embrace either. Finally, they broke apart, although their hands lingered on each other, not willing to let go. Their eyes met and Kara felt as though she was enveloped in a forest of emerald green. Their hands fell to their sides and the silence between them grew almost oppressive as they both resisted the urge to retake each other into their arms.

The silence was broken by a loud MEEEOOWW from inside the cottage. 

Lena sighed, “That would be Onyx telling me that it’s time for his breakfast.” Lena opened the door behind her and Onyx bolted past them through the tree line and into the forest beyond. 

“He’s so funny,” Kara smiled. 

“He really is. This is every morning like clockwork.” 

Lena turned her gaze back to Kara, “Are you hungry at all? I… I know you have a long journey back…” Lena’s sentence trailed off reluctantly. She didn’t know why but the thought of Kara leaving her made her heart clench in an unpleasant way. 

“Oh,” Kara said thoughtfully, “I guess I do. I should eat before I leave I guess.” 

“After breakfast we can sort out the matter of the curse you need to get Maxwell caught.” 

“Sounds good to me,” Kara lied as she smiled. She didn’t want to leave. As much as she knew she had work to do back in Willowdale, the thought of leaving Lena and the prospect of never seeing her again sat like a boulder in her stomach. 

“Do you mind if I bathe? I feel kind of gross,” Kara laughed and Lena’s eyes tracked a bead of sweat that rolled down Kara’s neck and into her shirt. 

“Absolutely go ahead,” Lena’s mouth dried at the thought that Kara would be naked in her home, but tried to keep her thoughts respectful. She failed, but she tried. 

They headed back inside and Lena pointed Kara toward the wash basin that doubled as a bath in the garden. She set to work gathering ingredients to make their breakfast. All the while, she kept Kara’s tunic on. Kara was back from her bath in no time and rejoined Lena in the kitchen as she was preparing their breakfast.  

Kara sat at the little makeshift table and quietly observed Lena as she moved. Every bit of her motion was comfortable and practiced. Kara rested her head in the palm of her hand and let her eyes lazily follow Lena around the small kitchen. Her raven black hair fell gently around her shoulders in loose curls and her bare legs looked like sculpted marble under her shorts. 

The scene felt so domestic to Kara it made her heart throb: Lena in her tunic and shorts making them breakfast in the quiet early morning light while the birds chirped outside, and the light streaming through the window making her pale skin glow and her hair turn into a halo of red-brown made Kara smile gently. Lena was so beautiful. 

Lena looked over to Kara and her heart nearly stopped. Kara was staring at her, chin in her hand with the sweetest smile Lena had ever seen laid gracefully across her face. Lena smiled and looked away. 

“What is it?” She asked quietly. 

Kara sat up, startled, “Oh- I… you’re just… really pretty. Sorry I didn’t mean to stare or make you uncomfortable I-“ 

“Kara it’s okay,” Lena’s smile lit up Kara’s whole chest as Lena padded quietly across the floor with a bowl of chopped fruit in her hands. She set it down on the rickety table and sat across from Kara. As they ate in silence, they both sat leaning over the table far more than was necessary, their elbows nearly touching. Lena’s knees rested between Kara’s underneath the table and both of their cheeks burned as they carefully avoided eye contact with each other while they popped blueberries and pieces of melon into their mouths. Their fingers brushed gently as they reached for the same piece of fruit, and Kara’s heart thumped hard as she wished for this moment to never end. 

Lena smiled as Kara gestured for her to take the fruit. She picked it up between her long fingers and gently beckoned Kara forward. Kara’s body obeyed without a second thought and she leaned even closer to Lena. Gently, Lena lifted the fruit to Kara’s lips and laid it softly on top of her tongue. Kara’s lips closed around the fruit softly and Lena could have sworn Kara brushed a feather light kiss against her knuckles as she retracted her hand. Kara’s knee bounced nervously under the table and brushed against Lena’s as it did. 

The two looked up at each other and held eye contact for a moment. It was almost as if some sort of magnetic force was working between them, drawing them in closer together until their foreheads were almost touching and they could feel each other’s soft breathing against their lips. 

Just as Kara thought their lips were about to meet, a loud, authoritative knock rang out against the door and the two jumped back from each other, breathing heavily. 

“Were you planning on having a visitor?”

“No. Not at all,” Lena whispered, “Go grab your sword.” 

The weight of Lena’s words dropped a rock into her gut and she nodded gravely. Silently, she crept around the table on the balls of her feet and withdrew her sword from its scabbard. The quiet drag of the metal against the leather sounded so loud to Kara as she watched Lena grab a knife from the table and hide it behind her back before opening the door. 

Behind the door were three mountainous men covered from head to toe in black metal armor. The man in front lifted his face guard. 

Lena made a disgusted noise, “Ugh, Maxwell. What do you want?” 

“Your mother would like a word with you,” his voice sounded slick and slimy like oil and it curled unpleasantly around Kara’s ears. 

“Okay, tell her ‘die.’ There, that’s one word.” 

“Speaking ill of the queen is punishable by death, you know,” he sneered. 

“Only if I don’t kill her first,” Lena shot back, and Kara thought the venom in her voice would be enough to kill Maxwell on the spot.

His hand shot out to grab her wrist, and Kara heard Lena mutter a quick incantation under her breath. The knife in her other hand started glowing a sickly green behind her back and she drove it between the plates of Maxwell’s armor on his arm. He howled in pain and fell backwards, black blood frothing and spitting from the wound where the knife was still glowing and buried. He writhed on the ground while the other two men behind him leapt forward to grab Lena and drag her out of the door. 

Kara leapt into action and ran from the cottage barefoot, jumping over Maxwell who was still writhing on the floor. The men had Lena on either side and Kara took a running start at the one on the left. With all her running force, Kara leapt into the air and leveled both of her feet against his knee in a vicious flying kick. Kara heard the sharp snap of breaking bone and creaking metal crumpling under the force of her impact. Kara may have been slightly smaller than the men in stature, but she was all dense, ass-kicking muscle. 

“Kara!” Lena cried as the man crumpled on top of Kara while he wailed in pain. He sat back on his heels and threw a punch with sickening accuracy into Kara’s jaw and Lena saw blood on the metal knuckles of his armor. 

She felt a deep pull in her gut as she willed all of her magical resources to manifest from the depths of her being. She imagined her magical power flowing up from her gut through her heart and out through the tips of her fingers. Bright green electricity flew forth from her fingertips and arced around the black metal of the two knights. It would have been beautiful if not for the sickening smell of burning hair and skin smoking from the chinks of their armor. The men screamed horrible, bone-chilling screams and fell back, jolting as residual electricity exited their bodies. 

Lena fell to her hands and knees, gasping as her heart pounded and her vision blurred. Her head throbbed and it felt like lightning arced through the backs of her eyes. She felt a hot stream of blood dribble from her nose and into the green grass, staining pieces of it black. Using exceedingly powerful spells like that one took a lot of her own energy to cast. 

“Lena!” Kara yelled as she scrambled across the ground over to Lena, her head still spinning from the impact to her jaw. 

Kara scooped Lena up in her arms and cradled her against her chest. Lena turned her face into Kara’s chest and tried to catch her breath. 

“Lena, are you okay?”

“Yeah, just powerful spells really take it out of me. It costs more energy to make something come into a physical being than it does to manipulate things that already exist.”

“Makes sense,” Kara breathed heavily and hugged Lena even harder into her chest. 

Maxwell made a terrible noise that echoed and gurgled unpleasantly against the walls of his helmet. Kara and Lena helped each other stand up and they both hobbled over to him. Lena glared down at his purple mottling face as the veins in his eyes slowly filled with black. 

Lena spit on the ground next to him, “I’d say tell my mother to go to hell, but it looks like she’ll just have to meet you there.” 

His eyes rolled back in his head and he stopped making sound. 

Still leaned into Kara’s side, Lena said, “My mother isn’t going to stop sending men after me. It’ll probably just get worse from here on out seeing as how someone leaked my location to Mother. I’ll have to leave.” 

Kara’s heart clenched, “Where will you go?” 

“The only place that my mother can’t touch without a powerful sorcerer: I have to go where the dragons are. They’re the only hope of evening out the battlefield. If they’re even really there. And if it is a real place, she’ll never expect me to venture that far. I’m tired of hiding. I don’t think I have much of a choice anymore. The only reason my mother would want me back is to use me for something.” 

“Well how will you get there?” 

“I have a friend in Nettle Heights who’s a fisherman. She can lend me a boat. She’s right on the coast, so I can leave from there.” 

Without hesitation, Kara said, “I’ll come with you.” 

“What about your sister?” Lena asked. She turned to face Kara, vision still blurred, and remained leaned heavily into Kara’s chest. Her heart pounded at the thought that Kara would risk her life for her, but shook the thought away. 

“I’ll leave her a letter to tell her the plan. We have a code we use to encrypt our messages. I’ll tell her where I’m going.” 

“It’s not even a real plan yet. I can’t ask you to do this for me.” Lena gave Kara another chance to back out.

“It’s not just for you,” Kara said assuredly, implying that it was definitely at least a little bit for her, “It’s for everyone. Omnis and its people can’t take much more of this constant destruction. If there’s even the slightest chance that I can save what would have been the next Krypton, or keep another forest from being ripped apart and burned down, then I have to do it.” 

“You’re a good person Kara,” Lena placed her hand over Kara’s heart, “You truly are.” Lena felt something slick and warm run over her hand and her vision finally cleared up and came back into focus. She could see that Kara had a large gash at least two inches long on the left side of her jaw that was dripping blood down her neck and onto her chest. 

“Oh my gods, Kara you’re bleeding!” Lena’s hand gently turned Kara’s head to the side to try to get a better look, but the wound was still bleeding too bad for her to see how deep it went. Hot blood ran like oil over Lena’s fingers, and panic spread in icy veins through her chest. 

“Ah, thought so,” Kara said as she winced. 

Lena hurried her back inside and made Kara sit down while she grabbed all of her medical supplies from the back room and a bucket of water from the garden in the back. Dark stains from her fingers smeared over the materials in her hands and made the handle of the bucket slippery. 

She dumped the water into the cauldron and lit the logs underneath it into a roaring inferno with a quick spell that only made her a little dizzy. She hurriedly sat down in front of Kara and pulled a clean rag out of her supplies and tried to soak up some of the blood that was still gushing from Kara’s chin. 

She cradled the other side of Kara’s face with her free hand as she gently dabbed at the gash. Red mazes of Lena’s fingerprints tracked and smeared the uninjured half of Kara’s face. It hurt, badly, but all Kara could think about was how fuzzy her head felt and how pretty Lena looked at that moment. 

The cauldron was boiling in no time and Lena pulled a small cup’s worth out of it. She dipped the rag into the cup and waited for it to stop steaming before gingerly pressing the rag into the wound. Kara winced and grit her teeth. 

“I’m sorry,” Lena’s breath shuddered roughly in her chest as she finished cleaning Kara’s face. Her hands caressed Kara’s jaw carefully, as if she was made of glass and not two hundred pounds of absolute warrior. Lena hastily wiped Kara’s face clean, tossed the rag to the side, and opened up a jar of green jelly-like liquid. 

“What’s that?” Kara asked, gritting her teeth through the pain arcing up her face. 

“Clotting jelly. It makes wounds stop bleeding. Another thing I invented.” Lena replied robotically as she worked with absolute precision and concentration, preparing the jelly along with a bandage. 

“Cool. You’re really smart Lena. Really smart and really pretty.” Kara said dazedly, her eyes roaming Lena’s face openly and lazily. Despite her panic, Kara’s gaze felt pleasant as it slid over her skin. 

“I think you may be feeling a little bit off, sweetheart.” Lena’s heart jumped in her chest as the soft pet name slipped accidentally from her lips. It felt so natural that Lena didn’t even think twice until the final syllable left her lips. Thankfully, Kara didn’t seem to notice and just nodded. 

Lena smeared the thick green liquid into the cut, and it instantly stopped bleeding completely. With the flow of blood stopped, Lena could see that the cut was approximately only an eighth of an inch deep. She breathed a sigh of relief and the panic released its icy hold on her chest. 

“It’s not that bad, but you may have a pretty big scar for a while.” 

“That’s okay, girls like scars.” Kara smiled. 

Lena laughed out loud at that, “I suppose that’s true, Kara.” 

“My head hurts.” She winced. 

“I’m not surprised, you did take a massive punch to the face.” 

“Mmhm feels like it.” 

“Let me finish putting a dressing on it and we’ll go from there, yeah? Your face is already bruising.” Lena touched the purpling skin of Kara’s jaw softly, her fingertips barely kissing the surface of her face. It was only for a moment, but Lena could have sworn she felt electricity skating down her fingers and into her arm.  

Kara nodded and Lena finished adhering the cotton padding bandage to Kara’s face with a skin-safe sap she’d discovered in the forest. While she was up putting the supplies away, she grabbed the rest of the bottle of alcohol that they hadn’t finished the night before. She passed the bottle to Kara, and she took it with a grateful sigh. She threw back a hefty swig and hissed as the liquid burned the back of her throat but relaxed as the alcohol’s numbing effect took action. 

“Thank you,” Lena said quietly. 

“For what?” Kara asked, “You’re the one that saved us with that badass green lightning.” 

“Just… Thank you for sticking with me and having my back. It- I haven’t ever experienced that before. So… thank you,” Lena folded her hands in her lap and lowered her gaze. 

“Of course Lena, I’ll always have your back.” 

Lena looked back up and made eye contact with Kara. Her face looked so sincere it almost made Lena want to cry. She knew in her heart that Kara meant those words with every fiber of her being. 

Kara suggested they move back to the bedroom so that they could both lay down for a little while before they made their plan. Lena agreed and helped Kara walk more than a little bit unsteadily to the bed. Her shoulders fit perfectly underneath Kara’s and Lena felt how sturdy her body was as Kara shifted her weight into her. Kara stumbled onto the bed, not bothering to shed her blood soaked shirt. Lena removed Kara’s one-sleeved tunic and slung it over the door. The two laid down next to each other like they had the night before, but this time there was no awkwardness between them and no worry about accidentally touching the other. Their knees and arms overlapped and if they were any closer they would have been holding each other. Kara stared dazedly into Lena’s eyes as if she was trying to see something. 

“What’re you thinking about?” Lena asked quietly. 

“You feel like a dream.” Kara responded easily, helped along by the alcohol and her head injury. 

Lena blushed, “How do you mean?” 

“You’re so beautiful in every way, it’s unreal. You went from skulls and black makeup and a lace dress yesterday to the softest, prettiest thing I’ve ever seen in my tunic and those ‘shorts’ things. Well, the only one I’ve seen in shorts but still. It just feels like a dream, I feel like I’ll wake up and you’ll disappear.” 

“I’m real, Kara. I’m here.” Lena reached out and gently touched the injured side of Kara’s face. It was bruising in earnest now, and Lena winced internally. 

Kara closed her eyes and sighed, “I know, that’s what feels so surreal.” 

Lena chuckled softly, “What would make me feel more real?” 

Kara’s eyes opened and focused on Lena’s face. 

“Come here.” 

Lena’s heart thumped heavily in her chest and she scooted closer until their chests were nearly touching. The material of the sleep shirts they both wore felt so thin to Lena at that moment. The light coming through the window in the bedroom lit Kara’s hair into a golden halo and sparked her blue eyes into fiery sapphires. 

Lena understood what Kara meant now, about everything feeling like a dream. Because in Lena’s mind, she was splayed out on a comfortable bed with the most beautiful girl she’d ever seen, practically holding her, bathed in golden sunlight, and the sight took her breath away. 

“You’re beautiful, Lena,” Kara whispered. 

“You’re breathtaking, Kara.” 

They remained still, encased in a bubble of warm yellow early-morning light, breathing barely an inch away from each other. Lena could see Kara’s pulse pound in her neck as she struggled to control her breathing. Kara could feel Lena’s soft breath ghost across her lips and she felt like she was suspended in the most heavenly torture imaginable. Every second Lena kept breathing without her lips on Kara’s was like an eternity, but she was terrified to shatter the moment and wake up from this dream. 

Just then, a low snore rumbled it’s way out of Kara’s chest. Lena smiled and brushed Kara’s hair back from her face. 

“Sleep well, Kara.” 

———

When Kara woke up she was wrapped in Lena’s arms with her head resting on Lena’s chest. She closed her eyes and tried to stay as still as possible. She’d been having the most wonderful dream, but she couldn’t quite remember what it was. She hoped she could go back to sleep to finish it. Lena’s arms tightened around her in her sleep and Kara smiled. She nuzzled her head further against Lena, and drifted peacefully back into her dreams. 

———-

Kara woke up to find that Lena was already getting them ready to go. Many traveling supplies were thrown together by the door, Kara assumed, to be sorted through later. More interestingly to Kara, though, was the fact that Lena was mid-change and was definitely not wearing a shirt. Kara didn’t mean to stare, but the art arcing up and down Lena’s entire back had her complete attention. She didn’t get a chance to look at the whole piece as Lena put on a fresh shirt, so she couldn’t tell what it was. She turned around and made eye contact with Kara, who tried desperately to act like she wasn’t just staring at her bare back. 

“Good, you’re awake,” she smiled, “We need to get ready to head out, the sooner the better. My mother will have already sent more men after me by now since Maxwell couldn’t send a hawk. How does your jaw feel?”

“Better, not as stiff. But it still hurts a hell of a lot.” Kara felt pain trace its way up her jaw as she spoke. That guard had to have been at least a half foot taller than Kara, and had at least a hundred pounds on her. She’d never seen a man so big in her life. 

“Good, I’m glad,” Lena smiled briefly before she swept her hair over one shoulder and continued pulling random supplies out for them to pack. With her hair moved to the side, Kara could see the very top of Lena’s tattoo, so dark on her pale skin, and it made Kara shiver. 

Kara tried to look like she wasn’t awestruck, “What’re you going to do about Onyx?” 

“Oh he’s coming with us. He’s a big baby; he would get sad if I left him here.” She turned around and picked out a little saddle bag from the closet, the perfect size for Onyx. She draped it around his shoulders and buckled the middle around his belly. He purred happily and rubbed against her knees. 

Lena looked Kara up and down.

“You can’t start a new journey with your clothes already ruined beyond repair.” She gestured to Kara’s torn shirt hanging on the door and the bloodstained collar around her neck. Lena turned to the closet yet again and pulled out a black canvas shirt, similar to her own white one, and handed it to Kara. Kara, unflinchingly, turned around away from Lena and whipped off her own shirt to change. Lena tried to stifle the gasp that filled her chest at the sight of Kara’s back. Lena could see the clear-cut lines of her back as she lifted her arms above her head, and it made Lena’s knees weak. Kara had her new shirt on and tucked into her belt in a few moments, so Lena didn’t get long to look, but what she did see made her want to see more. Kara turned around to face her and tried not to let Lena know that she saw her staring at her in the mirror. 

“Anyway, let’s get some supplies organized. I’ll have to grab some more things from my friend in town before we leave. We’ll need a lot if we're going to be crossing the Expanse.” 

Kara grabbed her pack and started grabbing the items that Lena pointed out to her until her bag wouldn’t physically carry anymore. When both of their packs were full of spare clothes and other essentials, they loaded Onyx with two full water skins on either one of his sides. It helped that he was nearly the size of a miniature horse, so the water didn’t seem to weigh him down at all. Kara strapped two sleeping rolls and a thick pelt over the top of her pack as well as a water skin of her own. 

Lena went to the garden to grab a small one-handed axe while Kara strapped her knife to her back on her belt and her sword to her hip, and they were ready to go in less than 20 minutes. The sun was just past its high point by the time they left the cottage. Lena led the way toward Nettle Heights while Onyx trotted at her side, and Kara tried not to think about the fact that she had never been this far away from home before. But she was comforted by Lena’s presence, though. They walked in a comfortable silence for a long time, the only sound between all three of them were Onyx’s soft chirps when he heard small things move in the trees. Kara couldn’t help but think that this would’ve been really nice if it weren’t for the circumstances. 

“So what’s your story, Kara?” Lena asked, finally shattering the quiet. 

“How do you mean?” 

“How’d you end up a blacksmith supplying the resistance?” Lena turned her gaze to Kara for the first time since they’d left. 

Kara’s eyes met hers and the words flowed out of her, uninhibited, “The army destroyed my village and killed my mother. My sister, Alex, she and her family took me in. Then her father, Jeremiah was killed in a surprise raid on our home. Alex joined the royal army as a spy for the resistance to get revenge for her father. She worked her way up the ranks to get close to the Queen. I became a blacksmith so that I knew Alex would have the best weapons and armor in the army and be safe. It also didn’t hurt that Alex’s military salary can pay for my shop ten times over and then some.” 

“That’s noble of you,” Lena said. 

“I suppose. I just couldn’t bear the thought of being out of the loop with Alex.” 

“If you could have been anything else, anything you wanted, what would it have been?” Lena asked. 

Kara thought for a long moment. 

“I don’t know, honestly. I’ve never really thought about it since the Danvers took me in.” 

“You’ll have to let me know when you think of it,” Lena said with a light smile. 

“I will. What would you have been?” 

“I think I would have wanted to be an inventor. I have a lot of ideas for new things but no means to execute my plans.” 

“I think you’re a great inventor now, even. Those shorts are awesome and that clotting jelly works like it’s magic. It isn’t magic right?” 

Lena laughed sincerely, “No it’s not magic, Kara, but I appreciate the compliment. It’s nice that you think my inventions are good even with my limited resources.” 

“I think you’re incredible,” Kara said quietly.

Lena blushed at the compliment and offered Kara a small smile before they continued on in silence once again. 

They walked for the remainder of the day until the sun had started to dip just below the horizon. That night was a clear night, where the wind was still and the animals chattered softly in the trees. It was Kara’s favorite kind of night. 

“It’s getting dark, we should make camp for the night soon,” Kara suggested, “How far is it to Nettle Heights?” 

“We should be there before the sun's peak tomorrow if we get up at dawn. It’s not much further now. Someone I know there should be able to point us toward a boat.” 

“Who do you know there?” Kara asked. 

“A woman named Lucy Lane. She’s a fisherman on the outskirts of Nettle Heights. She makes a killing on her fish prices, because she’s the only one brave enough to venture past the shallows to fish to get the bigger game.” 

“Brave or crazy?” 

“Maybe both, honestly. She hasn’t been quite right since her old partner, James, fell overboard a couple years back. He didn’t make it,” Lena said grimly. 

“That’s terrible,” Kara frowned. 

“Yeah. I know I wouldn’t go back out there, but she says it makes her feel closer to him.” 

“That’s what my mother used to say.” 

“About your father?” 

“Yeah. He loved the Expanse. He said he could feel it’s power when he touched it. I guess it was more powerful than he thought.” 

Lena put her hand on Kara’s shoulder and stroked her thumb over the fabric there. 

“We’ll keep each other safe. Neither one of us is going to die out there.” 

“MRROWW??”

“You too, Onyx. We’ll keep you safe out there, my baby boy,” Lena baby talked to him as she scratched his chin, eliciting a massive rumbling purr from his throat. 

They continued on for about three hundred yards and came to the edge of a small clearing, not unlike the one where Lena’s cottage sat. 

“Here looks perfect. And we’ll be able to see the stars tonight if the weather holds out,” Kara said. 

“Sounds good to me.” 

Kara set about unpacking their sleeping rolls which were strapped to the top of her pack. She laid them out next to each other and made another small pile of blankets for Onyx to lay down on. He walked circles around the padding and finally curled into a massive ball of fluff and shut his eyes. His flicking tail and twitching ears were the only signs that he wasn’t completely asleep. 

“I’m going to the edge of the clearing to gather some firewood. Do you want to come with me?” 

“I’d be happy to,” Lena smiled. 

The two spent around thirty minutes chatting and collecting pieces of wood until they had a stack that would more than last them the night. They stacked large flat rocks six inches up into a ring to contain their fire. Kara set the pieces of wood up in a sort of cone shape and piled thin dry twigs and kindling into the bottom of it. 

Just as Kara was about to set to work rubbing an ember out of a piece of spare wood, Lena held out her hand and closed her eyes. She muttered a quick incantation that Kara couldn’t understand a word of, and flames sparked to life in the center of the kindling. In no time at all they had a roaring fire to sit around.

“Ex Sanguinem et Corporem. It means ‘from the blood and body.’”

Kara gave Lena’s back a final gentle pat and released her so she could fix Onyx’s food. Lena retook her seat in the cot and carefully watched Kara as she sliced off a massive fillet for Onyx. He looked like Kara had given him the best gift he could have asked for and dragged it thankfully into the wash corner. He quickly began to chow down, paying neither one of them any more attention. 

Kara took a seat carefully next to Lena. “It’s still early in the afternoon; what do you want to do?” Kara asked quietly, giving Lena an out from the tension. 

“Hmm…” Lena hummed thoughtfully and lightly rested her head on Kara’s shoulder, “If you wanted to, we could work on your reading again.” 

“I think that’s a good idea. I want to be able to read your favorite books,” Kara smiled and left a brief kiss on the top of Lena’s head. Lena tucked her head under Kara’s chin so Kara couldn’t see the tears welling in her eyes. She’d never experienced unconditional affection from anyone before, and then there Kara was, making her feel like she was the only girl in the world that had ever had her attention. 

Lena and Kara tucked the rest of the fish away in a wooden box that Lena left a permafrost spell on so that the meat would stay cool and not spoil. Lena could have done without the extra layer between her and Kara’s skin, but if Kara wanted to cover up she wouldn’t stop her. The mattress still laid flat on the deck, so they elected to take in the sunlight and work outside this time. 

Kara flopped onto the bed and pulled her sketch pad, which also served as her reading notebook, onto the bed with her. Lena sat down with her and leaned into Kara’s side. For about three hours they practiced Kara’s reading rigorously, and Kara made tremendous progress. She had already memorized all the sounds that each letter made alone, and was well on her way to learning the sounds that each letter combination could make. Kara even managed to read the opening line of Lena’s horror novel, and it made a sense of pride swell so large in Lena’s chest that she thought she might burst. 

Lena suggested that Kara begin journaling about their adventure and how she felt throughout it as a way to practice her reading and writing skills, as well as help to relieve some of the stress of the journey. Lena also promised that she would never look in Kara’s journal without her permission, so she needn’t worry about what she wrote. Kara agreed and said that she thought it would be a good outlet for her stress as well. 

After another hour or two, Lena opted to get up and make them a quick dinner, while Kara stayed upstairs and tried her best to journal. 

 

Day 2

Lena is teeching me how to rite. I am not vary good, but Lena sayz that she is impresd. I will work hard and lern fast for her. She is makin diner now. I bet itz wunderfull wutever it is.

“A long, long time ago, in a kingdom far away, magic was forbidden from use.”

As she began reading more intently, she found the words coming to her more easily. She wasn’t perfect by any means, but she was making swift progress. 

“A knight from the royal guard was sent out into the forest to find and slay the evil witch that terrorized the kingdom.” 

Kara didn’t understand why people always wanted to kill witches when they were clearly just prettier and more talented than their peers; perhaps they were just jealous.

“Mrow.” He confirmed.

“Really?” The restrained excitement in her voice nearly bubbled over and her eyes brightened. 

“Yes, really, Kara.” Lena reassured her, “I still don’t understand why I feel everything that I feel, but I do understand that, at least. I love you.” It felt freeing to say those words, even if she wasn’t ready to say them in real life yet. It was Kara’s turn to tackle Lena into the bed of flowers that surrounded them.

She wished she could feel like this all the time. 

The tiniest, faintest voice at the very back of her mind whispered, “Maybe you could, if you would let her in for real.” 

“NO.” Lena’s mind silenced the small voice with a mentally deafening echo. Some things were too terrible to relive and she didn’t want Kara to know those things about herself. She’d told Kara about the Luthors making her kill many innocents, but this was different. It was personal. A deep, ugly scar far worse than those across her back. She locked the memory and the roiling feelings of sadness and hate deep inside her chest and boxed it away in the dark of her subconscious. She brought herself back into the moment where Kara’s arms were still wrapped tightly around her and Kara was still gazing at her with unbridled affection.

The leaves rustled softly and seemed to whisper gentle words into Lena’s ears as their sound passed by. “I don’t know how to tell you how I feel about you without making you uncomfortable,” Kara said suddenly.

“I mean… my feelings for you are a lot and I know your feelings are also a lot , and you don’t want to say anything in the real world yet, which is fine you barely know me. But I feel like this journey is dangerous enough that it justifies being honest with myself and each other as much as we’re comfortable. And I have so much that I want to say to you, Lena. But I don’t want to overwhelm you, but at the same time it feels overwhelming to keep it all bottled up inside and- and I feel like I’m rambling,” Kara’s words flew out of her mouth so fast that Lena almost had a hard time keeping up. Lena pressed a careful reassuring hand into the center of Kara’s chest and she felt Kara’s motion still under her touch. 

“Like we said, say anything you want in the dreams. It’s a perfect place to ignore reality and be completely indulgent. Even if the thought of everything that’s happening here happening in the real world makes me panic a little bit, I can’t deny my feelings for you, Kara. I love how your eyebrows crinkle when you’re thinking about something really hard, and I love how your smile lights up your whole face. Especially when I make you laugh. Lena’s skin burned pleasantly where Kara touched her and left behind a tingling sensation that lingered far longer than her touch. 

The wave of energy that Lena poured into Kara made her breath stutter in her chest and made her heart flutter wildly. Every cell in her body was alive with the feeling of Lena’s bright red light, and she struggled to keep air flowing into her chest. Kara didn’t know how her body could experience this much sensation without completely shutting down, but she was glad that she could. Kara couldn’t explain it, but she felt closer to Lena during this kiss than she had before. Even though their physical proximity was nearly the same every time they shared a kiss, she felt as though her heart was closer to Lena’s this time. 

They broke apart from their kiss to catch their breath, unnecessary in the dreamscape. The light streaming into the meadow lit Lena’s eyes into a green fire and captivated Kara. Lena thought that Kara must look closer to a god than anyone had ever looked before, her golden hair a fiery halo around her face. Lena’s fingers traced gentle patterns over Kara’s face and Kara closed her eyes to get lost in the sensation. Lena’s soft touch traced over her lips, her cheeks, the bridge of her nose, her chin, her forehead; everywhere she could touch, she did. Lena didn’t think she would ever get used to the soft electric feeling of Kara’s skin on her own, and she didn’t want to. 

Before long, the edges of the dream started to fade and both of them knew that their time here was coming to a swift end. 

“Will things change? When we wake up, I mean,” Kara asked. 

“What do you mean?” Lena raised an eyebrow. 

“Is it going to be too much when you wake up?” 

Lena knew what she meant: the closeness they had shared, the tender words, the vision of bringing a new life into the world together, it was all a lot to take in at once. 

“This last vision was… a lot of information to take in. I know what my feelings for you are, it’s just… a lot right now.” Lena struggled to explain. 

“It’s okay, Lee,” Kara offered her a soft smile and touched her cheek softly, “I understand.” Kara pressed one last kiss to her lips before the edges of the dream blackened and they were pulled back into wakefulness. Lena’s head rested on Kara’s chest. Kara’s hand stoked heavy, gentle lines down her spine and a light tune hummed through her chest into Lena’s ear. It wasn’t a song that Lena had heard before, but it sounded melodious as it shook through Kara’s broad chest. 

“What’re you humming, darling?” Lena whispered, eyes still closed. 

“An old song I used to hear in the village. It’s sweet. For lovers,” Kara blushed but her eyes didn’t break contact with Lena’s. Her bright energy wove its way through Lena’s chest and neither one of them had ever felt better. 

“You’ll have to sing it for me sometime,” Lena said quietly and pressed a kiss to her chest. “A lot has happened, do you want to journal about it for a little while?” She asked after a moment of silence. 

“I think that sounds like a great idea. I want to practice and get better so I can read with you, too,” Kara smiled brightly and pressed a kiss to the top of Lena’s head. 

“Then let’s get started.”

6. The Guide and The Guardian

Summary for the Chapter:

Kara and Lena reach their first destination of many. Answers only raise more questions, and they come to realize that this is a far greater undertaking than they had imagined.

Notes for the Chapter:

Explicit content this chapter. Can't say too much more this time, enjoy! :)

Day 5 

Ritting is reelly hard. I get frustratd eesily but Lena says that is OK. Lena is so nic. Shes always warm and soft and butiful.  I love her so much. It maks my chest hert.

When Lena opened her eyes she’d found that Kara’s were closed, but her hand ran up and down her back. They’d shared their ‘good mornings’ and Kara had taken plenty of time to cover every inch of Lena’s face in tiny rapid kisses. Lena suggested that she work on her writing and journal for a bit while she read her book. Kara had agreed and began diligently sounding out the words that she wanted on the paper.
