Lena’s lungs felt like fire in her chest as she struggled to catch her breath; her knees felt like they might collapse at any moment and her legs burned as if they were filled with venom. Even Kara, with her great strength and stamina, was thoroughly winded. 

 

When the fire in their bodies dulled and they were finally able to take in their surroundings, Lena and Kara looked up to the sky to search for any sign of the figure that had raced ahead of them. At last, Lena saw a distant speck of light hurtling into the clouds so many miles above them. Just as she lifted her finger to point it out to Kara, it flew into the thick grey clouds and disappeared. 

 

“Shit,” Lena muttered, thinking they had missed their chance at whatever they had been chasing that thing for. She looked back down to Kara who was still staring up at the sky in complete awe. Lena backtracked her gaze to the clouds and her jaw fell open in shock. Above them, a massive swath of the grey clouds was carving itself out of the sky to reveal the stars and the moons behind it. All four of the moons were completely full and shone a bright spotlight down into the basin of the mountain, almost as bright as broad daylight, but softer and paler. The tiny pond in the center of the bowl began to glow with a vibrant, inviting light that drew Kara and Lena’s gaze from the sky above. Without discussion the two started quickly down the stairs, ignoring the residual pain in their legs as the pool called to them. 

 

The stairs glowed softly under their steps as they descended, and the closer they came to the pond, the more it seemed like it was drawing them forward. Finally, they stepped into the bottom of the basin and drew close to the pool. Just as they reached the edge of the pool, the light glowing off the surface became momentarily blinding and the two had to shield their eyes. When the light subsided and the two could see again, they froze in shock. Before them rising slowly up out of the pool, was a beautiful woman with stark white hair as white as the four moons, and skin as black as the night with a galaxy of white constellations dotting every inch of her. The stars on her skin glittered like diamonds in the light of the moon. Her features were indistinct and ever changing, but invariably beautiful in every form they took. A regal crown of silver nine-point antlers sprouted from her forehead and glinted in the moonlight. When she had risen completely out of the pool, her feet seemed to float on top of the glowing water. Her eyes opened to reveal a void of swirling stars in each socket. 

 

She looked at Lena and Kara expectantly, but the two of them were completely dumbfounded and staring lamely back at her. She seemed to raise an eyebrow and opened her mouth to speak to them. 

 

“What have you come for?” Her voice was deep and smooth, soothing in a way. Lena found herself unable to answer, her words stuck in her throat. Kara was just as helplessly silent and the woman seemed slightly exasperated. 

 

“ What have you come for?” She asked more insistently. Lena managed to shake herself out of her reverie and stepped forward cautiously. 

 

“We- um- are here because we’re trying to, er, reach the other side?” The uncertainty in her voice made her statement sound like a question and the woman smiled, silver teeth glittering. 

 

“Humans are so predictable. I should have guessed your motive would be the same as the others. If you’re only here to satisfy your own selfish desires for power then I’ll have to send you back to where you came from.” She lifted her hand and her silver-tipped fingers began to glow with a deep blue light. 

 

“Wait!” Lena exclaimed and the woman paused in her motion. “We aren’t here for us. We’re here for everyone.” 

 

The woman eyed her, gauging the genuity of her statement. The light surrounding her hand petered out cautiously. “Go on,” she commanded and lowered her hand slowly. 

 

“The Luthors- Queen Lilian- they’re destroying everything and we have to stop them. They’re subjugating the people of the land to unimaginable cruelty, and destroying every forest, lake, river, stream- everything- anything that’s in their way. But the Queen has a dragon and a massive army. The resistance is respectable in the strength of its people, but they’re horribly outnumbered. We need help.” Lena spoke as quickly and concisely as she could, not wanting to upset the woman in front of her. 

 

The woman eyed them closely and seemed deep in thought for a moment. She stepped across and out of the pool toward the two and Lena and Kara instinctively stepped back from her aura of power. She reached out her hands and placed her fingers across the ridge of both of their foreheads. Everything in the world went black to Lena and Kara and the abrupt change made them gasp in shock. The blackness only lasted for a minute, and the two were returned into the light of the moons above them. 

 

“What was that?” Kara finally spoke, bewilderment evident in her voice. 

 

“I’ve looked through your thoughts. Memories, intentions, everything,” she explained simply. 

 

“Everything?” Lena asked, remembering every moment that she’d lived at the castle and every second that she’d shared with Kara. Her cheeks burned at the thought that this woman knew every thought and every intimate moment that the two had ever shared. 

 

“Don’t worry, humans are all the same. I’ve seen the mating rituals many times in all the travelers who have come here. Honestly, humans are very plain compared to the Celestials.” She spoke plainly, clearly having had this discussion before. 

 

“Celestials? Wait- who are you?” Kara asked finally. 

 

“I, personally, am the guardian of all the waters in this world. I guide the moons that pull the tides across the sky and am the representative of all things pure. I am the first guardian of the new world.” 

 

“The first?” Kara questioned, “So there are more of you- uh- Celestials?” 

 

“There are more entities that protect the life of this planet, yes.” 

 

“Are you gods?” Lena asked next. 

 

“No,” the woman shook her head, “The answer is much more complicated than that, though, by your human standards, by the magnitude of our power, I suppose you would consider us godly.” Their questions did not seem to annoy her, so Lena sought to satisfy her need for knowledge. 

 

“What is your name?” Lena asked. 

 

The woman looked surprised, as if it had been a long time since someone had asked her name. 

 

“My name… yes I did have one of those a long time ago, didn’t I? What did they call me…?” She wondered aloud and closed her swirling eyes in concentration. Her eyes snapped back open after a moment. “Selene. It’s not my full name, but it’s what you humans can understand.” 

 

“A beautiful name,” Kara commented quietly. Selene smiled at her compliment and nodded her head in acknowledgement. 

 

“Why did that misty thing bring us here, Selene?” Lena began her line of questioning again, gaining confidence. 

 

“Aura? She is one of my helpers. She guides travelers to me and calls to me when they are ready to be received.”

 

“Received for what?” Kara asked. 

 

“Judgement,” she answered simply but the word held an enormous weight in Kara’s ears. 

 

“Judgement?” Lena repeated. 

 

“Only those who are pure of mind, body, and soul are permitted to pass here. Filth is turned back to the disgusting shores of your home.” Her beautiful voice was filled with an uncharacteristic venom that made Kara’s knees quake. Upon noticing Kara’s terror, she smiled reassuringly. “Don’t worry, young one, you have already passed your judgement. I won’t allow those who fail to stay and ask questions.” 

 

“What do you do to them?” Lena asked, her fear bleeding out of her body and leaving a peaceful calm in its wake. 

 

“Like I said: those who fail are turned back to their polluted waters. I give them safe passage as the journey here is hard and dangerous. Although you two seem to have had a smooth trip here. Your protective spell is impressive, for humans that is.” It made a sense of pride swell in Lena's chest that she and Kara were able to impress this woman. 

 

“You said you’re the first guardian, right?” Kara asked and Selene nodded her confirmation, “How many more are there?” 

 

“There are innumerable Celestials in the universe, but the ones that you’ll be most interested in, there are only three including myself.” 

 

“If we have passed your test, what else is expected of us from here on out?” Lena asked. 

 

“Terra and Astraeus have their own tests of character. I’m not privy to any more information about those two than you are. It has been many millennia since Terra and I have spoken, and Astraeus makes it difficult to socialize with him.” She spoke as if she were a human gossiping about her friends and not an immortal being of immeasurable power. Kara thought the way she said Terra’s name sounded a bit jaded and wondered what had happened between them. 

 

“If you are gods compared to humans, couldn’t you solve the problem of the destruction and pollution of the land yourselves? No offense,” Lena added. 

 

“Unfortunately, Celestials have made a pact not to meddle in human affairs. Our version of ‘help’ for this planet would be sending a cleansing flood to rid the land of the humans who are destroying it,” Kara and Lena looked nervous, so she sighed and added, “Not that we would ever do that. There are far more good people in the world than there are bad, and it wouldn’t be fair to wipe out the good ones just to stop the bad ones. It’s up to humanity to decide if they want to live in a paradise or a wasteland. We Celestials will continue to live no matter what the humans do to that side of the world. It is beneath our power.” 

 

“That makes sense, I suppose,” Kara said quietly, and then asked, “The way you said Terra's name sounded… displeased. Do we need to worry about her trial?”

 

“No, she really is a very kind being. My quarrel with her is… personal.” Kara instantly recognized the tone of her voice as it was one she had heard many times back at home. Back when Alex and Maggie first moved in together and their lovers’ quarrels began in earnest. 

 

“How did you two come to meet?” Selene asked, clearly sensing Kara’s recognition. 

 

“Why?” Kara asked slightly defensively, “I mean you saw right?”

 

“Yes but I think it would amuse me to hear you say it,” Selene gave in to an entertained smile, and looked like she may even laugh. 

 

“Kara fell face first into me at my doorstep and I dragged her inside to my bed,” Lena recounted, smiling to herself. 

 

“Lena!” Kara whined. 

 

Selene laughed a loud sound like a crashing wave at Kara’s embarrassment. 

 

“Ah, thank you for humoring me. It has been many years since I’ve had something to chuckle at. It has been a rough century,” her teeth glinted when she smiled and Kara and Lena really thought she was a charming woman despite her terrifying power. 

 

“Any time,” Kara grumbled and scuffed her blood soaked shoe on the ground. Lena looked reflexively toward the sound and finally noticed the massive patch of skin flayed from the front of Kara’s shin. 

 

“Kara! What happened?” Lena asked worriedly and crouched to take a closer look. 

 

“I tripped going up the stairs earlier. Those are much sharper than they look. What is this mountain made of?” Kara waved Lena away, not wanting to look weak in front of this woman, though her shin did look worse to her now that she could see it clearly. 

 

“Onyx. Polished perfectly smooth on its faces by the sea.” Selene regarded the wound on her shin. 

 

“Oh Lena’s cat is named Onyx,” Kara mentioned offhandedly. Selene snapped to attention. 

 

“How big is he?” She demanded suddenly. 

 

“I don’t know, pretty big? Biggest house cat I’ve ever seen,” Kara answered, confused. 

 

“Allow me to assist you. Ion has been expecting you.” Selene waved her hand and some of the perfect water from the pool snaked out toward Kara and swirled around her wounded shin. It glowed brightly enough that it hurt Lena’s eyes for a moment and then fell away with a small splash. Kara’s shin looked perfectly smooth and free of wounds and scars completely. 

 

“Okay, firstly: Thank you, and that was amazing! Secondly, who is Ion? Why do you know about Onyx?” Kara questioned. 

 

“I’m not allowed to tell you anything further,” she said to her and then muttered more to herself than to them, “The Fated?” 

 

“Fated?” Kara wondered to herself. Her super sense of hearing was the only reason that she had heard Selene. 

 

“Here,” Selene said as she stepped forward again. She swiped her thumbs over their foreheads and Kara could see a protective spell, similar to the one Lena had given Onyx back in Nettle Heights, glowing softly. “This will give you safe passage and quick sailing to your next destination. You must move with haste. Ion has many things to show you. Goodbye.” She snapped her fingers and suddenly they were back on the deck of their boat watching the ocean crash back around Selene’s mountain, miles and miles away. The hole in the clouds began to close in on itself as the glowing light that had rained down on the mountain was sucked back up into the sky. It closed completely, and the blackness of the cloudy night draped itself over the world once more. 

 

“I guess Celestials don’t bother with the niceties,” Lena joked, “Quickly, let’s put the protective spell back.” 

 

Kara nodded and let her energy flow into Lena as she chanted her incantation. She’d gotten much better over the past weeks at letting her energy flow without a physical point of contact. It had started as just a suggestion for them to practice from Lena to satisfy her need to know if they even could. The purple light enveloped them once more just as the mountain was finally completely engulfed and disappeared back into the sea, leaving no sign that it had ever existed. 

 

“That raised a lot of questions,” Lena stated as she reattached the navigation spell to the wheel. It spun itself swiftly around and snapped to a stop when it reached the position that it wanted. 

 

“Yeah it did. Did you hear what she said? When she called us the Fated?” Kara asked. 

 

“No, I didn’t. What do you think that means? What’s going on here? I know she’s basically a god but there’s no reason for her to know about Onyx before having met us, and there’s no reason that someone named Ion should be expecting us when we only met a short time ago.” Lena continued on, speaking more to herself than to Kara. Lena had a burning thirst for knowledge, and being out of the loop frustrated her nearly to the point of anger. 

 

Kara shrugged her shoulders, but began to wonder to herself. It did sort of feel like fate that she had met Lena and fallen for her so quickly. And it felt like fate that they had undertaken this arduous journey together, growing stronger in their abilities both magically and physically as the days passed. Kara knew that Lena was still weary of the sudden magnitude of their feelings, so she kept herself quiet. 

 

“Hopefully we’ll reach the next landmark soon. Not that I’m not having a wonderful time with you, but I’m anxious to get on solid land for more than a few hours,” Lena admitted. 

 

“I agree. As comforting as your presence is, the ocean still terrifies me,” Kara said. 

 

“I hope these other guardians have more to tell us.” Lena’s brow furrowed as she wondered what other information was being withheld from her. 

 

“So do I. I don’t like being in the dark like this.” Kara crossed her arms and sighed a heavy breath as she looked up into the swirling purple mist that glowed into the blackness of the sky. 

 

The two fell into a tense silence as they speculated what was to come. Kara grew uncomfortable with the quiet and spoke up, hoping to lighten the mood. 

 

“I think Selene and Terra are having a lover’s quarrel,” she said frankly. 

 

Lena laughed out loud in shock at the sudden subject change and said, “What makes you think that?” 

 

“I know she didn’t say much about her, but the way she talked about Terra, the tone of her voice and the set of her face… It was exactly the same as when Alex and Maggie were fighting back at home. I just know it.” Kara said. 

 

“Maybe when we meet Terra she’ll be able to give us more information about everything,” Lena said with a chuckle. 

 

“I wonder if I’ll get to hear any more about what happened between those two,” Kara joked and Lena cracked a tired smile. 

 

“My whole body aches. How many stairs do you think we climbed?” Lena asked as she flexed her legs with a wince. 

 

“I have no idea, honestly. Had to have been at least a thousand. I’m impressed at your endurance ,” Kara said with a playful purr and a smile, knowing they were both too tired for any more physical activity that night. Lena smiled brightly at her, adoration plain on her face. 

 

“You are… so lucky you are so damn cute,” Lena laughed and shoved her shoulder playfully. 

 

“You know you like me,” Kara grinned at her and placed a gentle kiss on her forehead. Lena leaned into her side and suppressed a yawn behind her hand. 

 

“I think it’s time for bed,” Kara said before echoing Lena with a yawn of her own. Lena nodded tiredly and leaned more heavily into Kara’s side. Recently, Lena had taken to letting Kara carry her back to bed when they were both dead tired from a late night sparring session or any other strenuous activity on the boat. Kara bent down and wrapped one arm around Lena’s shoulder while the other swept her knees out from under her. Lena let her head fall softly against Kara’s chest and closed her eyes. 

 

Kara couldn’t help but stop and stare for a moment. Even after all this time with her, it was still hard for Kara to believe that Lena was real and not some sort of dream. Lena’s soft relaxed face bathed in the faintest purple light from their bubble made Kara’s heart race. It was hard for her to believe that Lena was really hers. She carried her quietly back into the cabin where Onyx was gazing at them from his pile in the corner. 

 

“Hey, Nyxie,” Kara murmured and he closed his eyes in response. It seemed as if he was waiting up for them and had taken the soonest opportunity to sleep. Kara laid Lena down onto the bed before crawling in after her and pulling Lena close to her chest. 

 

“Meet me in the same place?” Lena murmured tiredly, barely on the edge of consciousness. 

 

“Of course, Lee,” Kara placed another gentle kiss onto Lena’s forehead and she was already asleep. Kara smiled to herself before settling in and closing her eyes to concentrate. It had become almost second nature to wrap the threads of her magic around Lena’s heart and fall into her dreams with her. Soon enough the dreamscape came into view, and Kara fell through the thin veil of consciousness into the wakefulness of shared sleep. 

—— 

 

The two woke the next day tangled in each other’s embrace. The two frequently woke in this position, with Lena’s head resting on Kara’s arm facing her chest. Lena’s arm sling over Kara’s side and Kara’s other hand held Lena’s thigh across her own, tangling their feet together. Their morning began as usual, with a simple breakfast and Kara’s morning sword routine. 

 

“I wish we had a sword your size so you could practice,” Kara said as she resheathed her sword and propped it against the outer wall of the cabin. Lena sat with her back against the same wall where she had been watching Kara with rapt attention. 

 

“It would be more convenient. Though I wonder…” Lena trailed off as she began to try to think of a solution. Lena continued her train of thought as she watched Kara do her post routine stretch. She watched Kara wince as she stretched her sore legs. 

 

“How are you not sore yet?” Kara whined as she completed her stretch. 

 

“Mine always hits the worst the second day. I probably won’t be able to walk tomorrow,” Lena said with a grimace. 

 

“Aw and it’s not even for a fun reason like last time,” Kara said suggestively. 

 

“Very unfortunate, indeed,” Lena smiled and felt the magic well grow in her chest as Kara’s energy filled her. She had an idea suddenly. 

 

“Kara, do you think you would be able to continue to give me energy while we’re sparring?” She asked. 

 

“Maybe, if I concentrated on it pretty hard,” Kara replied. 

 

“If I can keep up a continuous source of energy, I may be able to create a magic field to extend the blade of my knife. It won’t be as long as a sword; I don’t think I’d be able to keep that up. But it’s better practice than none.” It seemed more feasible the longer that Lena thought about it and she was eager to try it out before she was too sore to move. 

 

“Are you good to sparr right now, or do you need a second?” Lena asked as she withdrew her dagger from its sheath. 

 

“I’m good, don’t worry,” Kara said with a smile and picked up her sword again. The two took up their positions across from each other on the deck as they always did. Kara reached out to Lena across the space with her magic, a feat in itself, and concentrated on keeping the link as she and Lena circled each other. Lena did her best to channel all of her energy along the blade of her knife. In a few moments a purple jet of razor sharp energy sprung from the tip of her blade. 

 

“Great!” Kara exclaimed, “I can teach you short sword basics. It’ll be a little different with a full blade.” Lena nodded and the two got to work in earnest. Lena picked up this style of fighting just as quickly as she had with her knife, and Kara was soon battling back excitedly. Kara threw a wide slash that she knew left her wide open for a deflection and was delighted when Lena took the opportunity to let Kara’s blade slide off of hers to the side and cornered her against the railing of the boat. Lena’s flaming blade was inches from Kara's throat. 

 

Kara grinned widely and was just about to congratulate Lena on her win when she saw a dark speck on the horizon behind her. Her eyes squinted harshly as she tried to make out its shape. 

 

“What is that?” Kara asked, pointing over Lena’s shoulder. 

 

Lena turned around and followed Kara’s gesture to the horizon. She cut the flow of power to her blade and redirected it to her eyes to enhance her vision momentarily. Lena was surprised Kara had even seen it at all from this distance as it was still hard to make out even with her spell. As far as she could tell, a large mountain far far off in the distance sat squat on the horizon waiting for them to arrive. 

 

“Is that the second landmark?” Kara asked. 

 

“I think so,” Lena said. She couldn’t tell if she wanted to feel excited or trepidatious. If this Celestial was any less friendly than the last, then they may be on a quick trip back home far sooner than expected. 

 

“Gods, I hope we can land soon! I hate being out at sea like this,” Kara frowned and Lena grabbed her hand. 

 

“We’ll land soon enough, darling,” she said with a smile, “I’d estimate that we’ll reach the landmark no later than sunrise tomorrow.” 

 

Kara smiled and finally offered a belated congratulations on her win. Lena laughed lowly and stepped in close to press her hand against the broad plane of Kara’s chest. Kara made a joke that winners should receive prizes and Lena’s breath caught in her chest at the route her mind took. 

 

“And just what is my prize, Daddy ?” Lena asked as she smiled sweetly up at Kara. The word flipped a switch in Kara’s mind and she scooped Lena up with a snarl and led them back to their bedroom. 

——

 

 Kara woke early the next morning, unable to sleep in her excitement to meet the next Celestial. She wondered what Terra would look like and if she was nice. Lena slept soundly across her chest, Kara’s heartbeat keeping her soundly in her dreamworld. Kara ran her fingers gently through Lena’s hair as her excitement and anticipation for the day grew with each passing minute. 

 

Soon the sun crept over the horizon and laid its soft fingers across Lena’s bare back. The gentle warmth woke her and she breathed in deeply the scent that Kara always carried with her. Fresh and foresty; it reminded her of her cabin back home. Lena opened her eyes and blinked them a few times until they’d adjusted to the light. 

 

“Good morning.” Lena pressed a kiss to Kara’s chest and ran her hand up and down her torso. 

 

“Good morning, Lo- uh- Lena,” Kara stuttered on her words and tried her best to let it pass without incident. 

 

Lena either didn’t notice or ignored the near slip and sat up to press a kiss to Kara’s lips. Lena knew she would never get tired of this. The way the light lit up Kara’s eyes, the way the sun felt on her skin, the way she could feel the goosebumps form on Kara’s chest as her breath skated across Kara’s skin, everything was absolutely perfect every single time Lena awoke in Kara’s arms. 

 

Lena sat up to straddle Kara at the waist, with innocent intentions only, and leaned down to press a loving kiss to Kara’s lips. She opened her eyes and Kara’s face, so open with love and adoration and kindness and patience… Lena could hardly stand it. She almost brought herself to say it, too. Those words. The ones that she couldn’t ever say without something being ripped away from her. But she loved Kara so much more than anyone else from her past. It would hurt so much worse. It would kill her to have Kara ripped away. So she wouldn’t. Not for now. Not until she was sure it was safe and nothing bad would happen. She pressed another swift kiss to Kara’s cheek and sat all the way up, her hands resting on Kara’s bare stomach. 

 

“Do you want to go see how close we are to the next landmark?” Lena asked, hoping to relieve the romantic tension of the moment. 

 

Kara’s eyes lit up and she nodded excitedly and sat up with Lena still in her lap and wrapped her in a hug. It was positions like this that made Lena realize just how tall Kara was. Even with Lena sitting on top of her thighs, Kara was still slightly taller than Lena at this moment. Lena loved the security that she found in Kara’s massive size and how every hug Kara gave her completely enveloped her. Despite their dangerous undertaking, it made Lena feel safe. The two got up and got dressed quickly. Kara wrapped her sword to her waist with a makeshift belt that she’d made from a piece of rope so Lena could have hers. Lena readied her knife at her hip and the two patted Onyx good morning and walked out onto the deck. 

 

It was slightly jarring to see how close they’d come in the night to the landmark. It wasn’t more than a mile or two away now, and it was even more massive than the last island. This island had green trees and waterfalls running all over its surface. Closer to the base, Kara could see swirly red and tan rocks peeking out from between the trees, and something glittering at the very edge of the shore that she couldn’t quite make out at this point. 

 

“Gods- Lena we’re almost there!” Kara exclaimed excitedly and tapped Lena rapidly on the shoulder. Lena smiled and patted Kara softly on the back twice. She was excited about the prospect of gaining some more knowledge of their situation, but also had a feeling that this would not be as easy as the last trial despite Selene’s promise that Terra was kind.

 

Lena got Onyx breakfast first and then readied theirs. Kara and Lena sat on the deck for the remainder of the morning discussing what they may find over breakfast. Lena took their bowls downstairs and washed the fish and rice out of them before retaking her place at Kara’s side on the deck. They were only about a quarter of a mile from docking at this point, and Kara could see that the glittering that she’d been unable to make out before was actually millions of clusters of multicolored gemstones scattered all around the shores on the island. 

 

Kara excitedly pointed out the beautiful colors to Lena and her mind wandered back to the beautiful ring she’d put on Lena’s hand in the vision before. She wished she could deliver something similar someday. She didn’t have too long to sulk before the world around them began to rumble and shake. Kara grabbed Lena’s hand and pulled her close against her chest. The mountain in front of them sat up its massive head that had been invisible to them before and regarded them over its shoulder. It blinked slowly at them with its cold stone eyes before turning and reaching out with a massive moss covered hand to pick up their tiny little ship between its fingers.

Notes for the Chapter:
