7. The Giant and The Gemstones

Summary for the Chapter:

Lena and Kara make a new friend on their journey.

Notes for the Chapter:

Okay so /TECHNICALLY/ tw for fake major character death but I promise it's all good. Tw for blood also.

“Holy shit!” Kara screamed as their ship, still encased in its bubble, careened into the air. Kara’s feet came out from underneath her as she tumbled to the deck, and she quickly slapped her hand down on the deck to keep Lena from hitting her head as she fell after her. Two massive mossy stone fingers pinched the top and bottom of the bubble and lifted it to the giant's eye level. Their ship was about as long as the iris was wide, and the rock of its eyelids sliding closed was deafening. Kara grit her teeth against the scraping sound as she tried to help Lena stand. Kara and Lena struggled to their knees as their ship canted unevenly between the mountainous fingers. Just as Kara managed to get one of her feet back underneath her, the giant swung their ship back from its face at arm’s length. 

 

“I’m getting really tired of getting thrown around!” Lena yelled as she tumbled back onto the deck. She could hear Onyx howling extremely unhappily in the cabin and worried for his safety. 

 

“What do we do?” Kara yelled back. Lena didn’t get to answer before the giant opened its cavernous mouth and began to screech nonsensically at them. Kara slapped her hands over her ears and collapsed onto the deck. Her enhanced hearing made the intense noise rattle around violently in her skull, and it felt like her head was splitting open. Lena scrambled to her side and held her close, knowing that she couldn’t do anything else to help at this point. The screeching was reverberating uncomfortably in Lena’s own ears, so she couldn’t imagine how painful it was for Kara. 

 

It seemed to notice Kara’s pain and stopped screaming momentarily. Kara hesitantly brought her hands down from her ears. The giant looked confused and screeched slightly more gently at them. Kara clapped her hands back over her ears and writhed in pain on the deck. Her head throbbed, and Kara thought she may actually go deaf. The huge creature shut its mouth with an even more deafening crash. It almost looked vaguely concerned. As concerned as it possibly could with its mottled stone features. The massive creature raised its free hand to scratch its head, seemingly in deep thought. The motion of its hand sent fully grown trees and boulders flying from the top of its head splashing into the water around it. Lena couldn’t imagine how huge this creature had to be to stand in water this deep. 

 

Kara cautiously removed her hands from her ears and took a few steadying breaths. Her ears were ringing loudly, and the sound around her was muted in comparison, but nothing irreparable had happened. She laid her hand across Lena’s on her shoulder. and the two rose to their feet together. The giant raised its free hand again and pointed to the two of them. Kara and Lena looked at each other, confused.  

 

“Is it… talking to us?” Kara whispered. 

 

“I think so?” Lena murmured back. 

 

It pointed again at them and then pointed to its head. It repeated the motion again and moved their boat, more gently this time, toward the shore at the base of its chest. Kara and Lena managed to keep their footing as they descended. It set their boat down as gingerly as it could, and their protective spell dissolved into millions of flakes like it had at the previous island. This close, Kara could see all the innumerable gemstones encrusting the base of the mountain and was awestruck by their beauty. The mountain turned its head and tipped it back down, chin resting on its chest. Its eyes closed with a final scraping crash, and the giant became immobile once more. As the island settled back into itself, a waterfall began to pour down from the back of its head and flowed down over its face. The running water almost looked like tears at the corners of its closed eyes. It was devastatingly beautiful in the sense that Kara knew she would never see anything exactly like this ever again in her life. She tried to memorize everything about the truly magnificent sight in front of her. 

 

Lena turned to Kara, shaking her out of her thoughts, and said, “I think it wants us to go to the top?” 

 

“I think so. Sure would be nice to have a guide this time like we had with Selene. The path up this mountain looks a little bit less clear cut,” Kara said as she searched for an easy way to climb to the top. The hike to the top would be at least a two thousand foot gain in altitude, and most of the terrain looked wild and treacherous. Kara and Lena went into the cabin first to find a very ruffled and displeased Onyx replacing all of his blankets into his pile. They had been strewn about the room and he was in the middle of dragging one back into his corner when they found him. He glared at them like he was criticizing their boat driving skills before ungraciously spitting the edge of the blanket out of his mouth into a heap on the floor. 

 

“Okay, we’ll help, jeez. What do you have an attitude with me for? I’m not the one that picked up the ship!” Lena spoke to Onyx as if he was a small human and not a large animal. Kara found it endearingly adorable and smiled to herself as she helped Lena collect Onyx’s blankets while he watched scrutinously from the corner. Once all of the blankets were placed to his liking, he spun around three times on the top before sinking into the pile with a huff. He closed his eyes irritatedly and tried to resume the nap that he had been so rudely awakened from. 

 

The two exited the cabin and Kara leapt over the side of the railing about eight feet down to the ground. She landed gracefully on the balls of her feet and absorbed all of the impact expertly with a bend of her knees at the exact right time. There wouldn’t have been much impact in any case with the soft silty sand on the shore. 

 

“I hope you don’t expect me to do that, Kara,” Lena crossed her arms at the top of the railing. 

 

“Just jump. I’ll catch you, Lee,” Kara opened her arms and smiled up at her. Lena couldn’t argue with that smile and knew intrinsically that Kara would never let her fall. She swung her legs over the railing and jumped easily off the side. As promised, Kara caught her skillfully in her arms without so much as even a grunt of effort. Lena stretched up to plant a kiss on the side of Kara’s neck and hopped down from her embrace. Kara smiled and grabbed Lena’s hand to lead the way. 

 

They walked around the base of the island for a few minutes, searching for a way up. They saw hundreds of variations in the millions of gems and even more varieties of plant life at just the base of the mountain. Kara couldn’t see them, but in the trees further up the mountain she could hear the thousands of skittering feet and chittering voices of the animals on the island. At last, they came to a thin strip of a rocky trail that seemed to run in a jagged line up the mountain giant’s spine. Kara took point, making sure that there were no loose footholds or ankle breaking fissures that they could fall into, and they began their ascent. They didn’t speak much, both of them saving their breath for the climb. 

 

Lena grimaced as the burn set into her sore legs early on. The second day of soreness was always the worst for her, and this time was no exception. She did her best to keep up with Kara, but about a quarter of the way into their climb she had to ask for a brief reprieve. 

 

“I’m sorry,” Lena said between gulps from her small canteen strapped to her belt, “My legs are killing me from our last climb.” 

 

“Don’t be sorry, Lena. It’s okay,” Kara smiled down at her and kissed the top of her head. They rested in silence for just a moment until they heard a heavy rustling in the bushes coming nearer to them. Kara dropped into a fighting stance and Lena swiftly drew her knife. Their fright was for nothing as they saw a small fat stack of stones pushing its way through the bushes onto their little trail. The stack of stones resembled the cairns that Kara had seen lining the trails between the villages, but with limbs and a head. Its little legs were made up of smaller half-palm sized stones while its belly was made up of three big flat stones that made it appear to have tummy rolls. Its little head sat squatly on its fat body and two divots in the rock seemed to indicate where its eyes may be. 

 

As it finally pushed its way through the bush, its little ankle caught on a root and it tumbled over, scattering its parts in front of them with a soft clatter on the dirt. Kara immediately dropped to her knees and began frantically restacking the little man from his ankles up. 

 

“Oh my gods- are you okay little guy?” Kara carefully placed its head back on its body, and the little man lifted his arm components to twist his head back on the right way round. He nodded to them wordlessly and turned around. He began walking up the trail in front of them very slowly, his little legs only carrying him about two inches with every step. 

 

“Do you think it’s a guide? Like Aura?” Lena whispered into Kara’s ear. 

 

“I think so. It looks like he’s taking us somewhere.” 

 

Kara and Lena followed closely behind at a languid pace. Lena could sense Kara’s slight frustration that they couldn’t move faster every time she had to take one step and wait five seconds to take another to keep pace with the little man. Lena was grateful for the slow pace, but it meant she had to be up on her sore legs for far longer than she originally had planned on. Eventually, the little man turned sharply off of the trail onto an even narrower strip of dirt that forked off to the side. 

 

“Careful, watch your feet,” Kara said over her shoulder to Lena. She nodded, and they continued following the stack of stones into the trees. All around them, Kara could see flitting glimpses of all the wildlife skittering around them. She caught flits of blue plumage, green fur, black scales, and even stranger things that her mind couldn’t even begin to comprehend. The creatures seemed to be avoiding them, though, so she never got to see anything clearly. After a while the little man came to the side of a large boulder and stopped. He turned around, almost as if to check that they were still behind him, and upon seeing that they were, he placed his stubby hand on the surface of the boulder. 

 

Little glowing veins of yellow light spiraled out from his hand in bright ruins across the surface. Lena recognized some symbols from her books back at the castle, but many of them were completely foreign to her and much older than the texts in her books. As the light faded from the lines, the rock started to crack and crumble apart. As the boulder fell away in front of him, a cavern tunneled deep into the center of the mountain was revealed. As the cairn stepped through the entrance, light spilled out from beneath his feet and climbed like ivy up onto the walls and ceiling, illuminating the tunnel in a weak yellow glow just bright enough to see in front of them. He continued taking his tiny steps down the tunnel and Kara and Lena fell in step behind him. The boulder that had crumbled to reveal the tunnel reformed itself with a loud clatter and sealed the entrance off from the outside world. 

 

The chattering noise of the animals on the island fell away and became muted by the cave wall, and Kara reveled in the fact that she could hear comfortably again. Her ears still held a residual ringing from earlier, and it was soothed by the silence of the cave. His tiny feet clacked quietly on the cave floor, muted in comparison to the slap of Kara and Lena’s shoes behind him. They continued walking behind him for what felt like an hour and finally reached a massive open space carved out directly from the center of the mountain. The room itself was a massive dome made of the same mottled rock that they had seen on the outside and dotted all over with little glittering gemstones of various vibrant colors. The colored gems cast a rainbow of light across the walls of the cavern, and it stole Lena’s breath away.

 

Above them, the ceiling of the cavern turned into a clear crystal material that let a refracted flood of natural light stream into the cave. The cave walls were so clustered with gems that Kara could actually see the light slowly twinkling off of the facets alternately as the sun shifted its position minutely in the sky. Lena grabbed Kara’s arm and pulled herself close to Kara’s side as they walked at a snail's pace behind the cairn. It felt nice to have this moment of stillness in the air around them. 

 

It felt peaceful enough that Kara reached out to Lena with her bright blue light. Lena felt Kara’s energy and let her own flood into Kara in return. It was nice to share a moment of peace like this in the waking world on solid land. It almost felt like one of their dreams with their beautiful surroundings and the tiny man walking slowly in front of them. Lena leaned heavily into Kara’s shoulder. The little man led them to the other side of the cavern where a small square platform about five feet across raised slightly out of the ground. Above the platform, a tunnel cut deep into the rock of the mountain rose straight above them. The cairn stepped onto the platform and gestured for them to follow. 

 

Kara and Lena stepped onto the platform behind him. The little man folded his legs and sat down to place his nubby hands flat on the floor. Little runes filled with light snaked out from his hands again across the platform, and it began to rise out of the ground toward the tunnel above them. When the platform met the bottom of the tunnel they were cast into darkness save for the light that came from the glowing ruins on the platform. The darkness lasted for about ten minutes. Ten minutes of near complete silence in complete darkness save for the glowing ruins, huddled close together on the small platform, and Kara felt completely safe for the first time since she’d set foot on the boat. Lena’s presence was comforting enough, but the threat of the Expanse was too great for her to completely ignore, no matter how much she trusted Lena’s protection spell. Here, in complete safety, Kara felt free to let her magic dance through Lena’s chest happily and drew her close in a hug. 

 

Lena clung to Kara like she was afraid if she let go this moment would end. She didn’t want to go back to the real world where they were risking their lives. She didn’t want to go back to the real world where strangers were waiting for her and godly entities knew things about her that she didn’t. She wanted to stay in Kara’s warm safe embrace forever. Unfortunately, their peace came to an end as a panel above them opened and bathed them in blinding natural light. Their platform rose to the top of the tunnel, and they were left standing on a small landing about nine tenths of the way up the spine of the mountain. The trees here were sparser, and about two hundred feet ahead of them on the trail, the mottled stone turned abruptly to the clear crystal that they’d seen capping the cavern on the inside of the mountain. 

 

The little man stood up and shooed them off of the platform with a gentle push on their shins. Kara and Lena stepped back off the platform and he sat back down, placing his hands back down on the surface. The ruins glowed again and the slab lowered back into the spine of the mountain, taking the rock man with it. The slab that had opened at the top to let them exit flipped back over the hole and seamlessly sealed it off. The two exhaled their disappointment at the sudden change in scenery and began to walk up the remaining length of the trail to the top. The trail was wider and significantly flatter than their previous steep descent, and the two walked quietly side by side, still sharing their magic between them. Their arms brushed lightly, and the trees came to an abrupt end around them as they reached the top. 

 

The mountain plateaued at the back of the giant’s head. A pool connected to a small river set in the crystal poured the waterfall off the back of its head into the sea below. Standing waist deep in the water, a young woman who looked no older than twenty-five floated waist deep in the pool with her eyes closed. Her dark green mossy hair fell in waves over the dark brown skin of her shoulders and chest, dangling into the water. Her hands were flat on top of the water at her sides, and Lena could see a faint glow emanating from her fingertips. As they got closer, Kara and Lena could both discern that she was incredibly beautiful, much like Selene, but her features were concrete. Her face was soft and round and she looked very sweet. When they came just within fifteen feet of her, her eyes snapped open revealing molten fiery orange swirling inside. Kara and Lena immediately slowed their pace to a crawl. 

 

She began to wade toward them in the pool and rested her forearms on the ground outside. She laid her head to the side on top of her arms and merely observed them as they crept forward. She seemed to suppress an amused smile. When they finally came within a respectable talking distance, the woman began to speak. 

 

“What are your names, strangers? I apologize for the volume of my voice earlier. It seems one of you has sensitive ears.” Her voice was quiet but powerful and deep, like an earthquake threatening to break loose in her chest. 

 

“You were the mountain giant?” Kara asked. 

 

“Yes, that’s correct,” she replied softly. 

 

“Why are you… different now?” Kara asked cautiously. 

 

“Celestials can choose what form they take. My go-to is my giant form. It is what is most comfortable. However this form suits me just as well, and it seems to suit you better.” She spoke matter-of-factly as if this were an everyday conversation. Kara supposed that for her it was. “Now, your names?” She got back to the topic at hand. 

 

Lena cleared her throat and spoke first, “My name is Lena.” 

 

“Lena what?” The woman asked coyly. Lena had a feeling that she already knew the answer. 

 

“Lena- erm- Luthor.” She mumbled her last name, clearly ashamed and embarrassed. 

 

“Mmhm, I see.” The woman sounded only slightly displeased. “You know, you can be ashamed of the things that your predecessors have done and still say your name proudly. You are more than the weight of your name, and you have extraordinary potential.” Lena looked slightly shocked at her words. How could she possibly know anything about her life? She addressed Kara, “And your name?” 

 

“I’m Kara Danvers,” Kara replied sheepishly. 

 

“Is that your only last name?” She clearly already knew the answer, but who was Kara to deny her?

 

“No… My real last name is Zor El. But I haven’t been called that in years.” Kara frowned, remembering all the unpleasant things that had happened in her past. 

 

“Mm,” she hummed in acknowledgment, “And I assume your cat is on the boat?” She asked. 

 

“Yes- are you Terra?” Kara asked her. 

 

“Mhm. Ion’s information told me I should be expecting you soon. Not much else though.” Terra shrugged. 

 

“It seems like this Ion told you more than they told Selene,” Lena commented, “She didn’t know our names beforehand.” 

 

“Hm. I assume that’s my fault. Astraeus passed information from Ion to me. I guess I was a little sparse with the information I gave her,” she turned her nose up haughtily and huffed, “Not like she’s bothered to talk to me at all in the last two centuries.” Kara tried not to break into a grin while one twitched at the corner of her mouth.

 

“Called it,” she thought to herself. 

 

“Is there an… issue?” Lena asked cautiously. 

 

“No… nothing aside from the fact that she’s being petty and childish.” She crossed her arms across her chest and the water splashed in her wake. The two stood quietly for a long time before Kara felt like she should say something. 

 

“Do you want to t-“ 

 

“Yes! Thank you! Astraeus is so stoic and hard to talk to and Selene is being a complete jerk! Ion is far too busy with work for small talk either.” Kara supposed Terra was the open type. “And for what?! Because that nymph was checking me out? That was two-hundred years ago! And besides, everyone checks me out! Earth magic carries powerful sexual energy; it’s like a pheromone! Everyone knows that!!” Terra threw her arms up in the air and small cracks formed in the crystal under them. 

 

“Yeah of course, everybody knows that,” Kara said, having learned a new fact. Lena nodded like she actually knew that. 

 

“Thank you! I tried to tell her that, but she wouldn’t have any of it! She won’t even talk to me. She didn’t even say thank you when I gave her the information.” Her pretty face fell into a slight frown and Kara thought for a moment that magma tears might start dripping from her eyes.

 

“Why won’t she talk to you about it?” Lena asked. 

 

“I don’t know,” she replied sadly, “We’ve been together since I can remember.” 

 

“Maybe she's just hurt,” Kara replied, thinking of how it would make her feel to have someone looking at Lena the way she did. 

 

“Maybe. But I told her again and again that I’ve thought nothing about another being since we joined. I don’t know how to fix it.” 

 

“How do you communicate? I mean with the distance between your islands.” Lena asked. 

 

“Celestials can migrate from one landmass to another with enough energy. I’ve visited several times in the last two centuries, but she’s been cold every time. Dismissive almost.” Terra frowned. 

 

“Maybe she just needs more reassurance. I would be very hurt if someone looked at Kara like that,” Lena said. Kara felt her heart flutter a little bit at the thought that Lena would be jealous when it came to her. 

 

“I suppose. I’ll have to pay her a visit once you two take your leave.” She seemed to shake off the unpleasant thought and got back to her Guardian duties. “Ion is expecting you soon, but you cannot physically pass this barrier without passing judgement.” She gestured to the mountain as the barrier to which Kara and Lena nodded. 

 

Terra lifted herself out of the pool, and the two discovered that she was completely nude. The two quickly averted their gazes, both of their faces coloring a dark red. Terra wrung out the ends of her hair and threw them back over her shoulders, leaving her entirely bare. She laughed loudly at their embarrassment. 

 

“You humans are so funny! So prudish. As if you weren’t born naked or something,” she laughed once more and regained her stern composure. “My trial isn’t as kind as Selene’s. It is the ultimate test of fortitude. You will have to prove that you are steadfast, willing to take on any amount of pain and hardship to achieve your goal. I am not allowed to give you many specifics, but you are hurtling toward a task far greater than you originally planned upon. You must prove you are committed no matter what. Are you willing to accept these terms?” Terra’s face was grim. Kara and Lena could tell that she took no joy in this task. 

 

Kara and Lena looked at each other for guidance. They locked eyes and found nothing but surety within each other. Kara and Lena could tell they were both scared, but they also knew the value of their own mission. Although the magnitude of their situation was ambiguous at the moment, it was clear that it was important enough for them to persevere. Kara clasped Lena’s hand and pulled her closer to her side. 

 

“We accept the terms.” Lena’s voice was steady but Kara could feel the tremble in her hand as she spoke. 

 

Terra nodded and sighed, “Very well. I do not enjoy this. I hope you won’t think of me as an enemy for it. But my trial will make you experience pain so great it has the potential to break your spirit. You cannot stop the trial. Should you break, you’ll fail and I’ll send you back to your home.” Lena’s hand clenched in Kara’s grasp. Kara's pulse thundered in her chest as she wondered in horror what she was going to experience. Lena felt an impending panic attack building in her chest as she clung to Kara’s arm. 

 

Terra lifted her hands, which began to glow a bright green, and stepped toward them. She placed the pointer and middle fingers of both of her hands in the center of their foreheads and their vision went black. Swiftly, Kara found herself thrust onto a bloody, smokey battlefield, sword slick with blood and drawn in her grasp. Around her, countless bodies in piles littered the field. On closer inspection, the bodies piled around her were her friends and family: Alex, Eliza, Lucy, everyone she knew, dead around her. Kara felt like her stomach was full of lead and her chest was full of fire. She felt like throwing up. She was angry. She was shocked. She was hurt. She couldn’t comprehend the gut wrenching pain filling her whole body as she gazed at the corpses around her. Finally, her gaze fell onto another body, much smaller and more familiar to her than the others, lying on its own in the dust. 

 

“No. Please. Please gods no-“ Kara crept closer and felt her stomach drop to her knees as the air was sucked out of her body. Laying in the dust, a massive wound on her side pouring blood into the grit, a pale, barely conscious Lena struggled to open her eyes and look at Kara. Kara sprinted to her side and slid on her knees through the dust, picking Lena up and cradling her against her chest. Lena’s eyes lolled in her head, rolling around trying to focus on Kara. Her blood turned the dirt black and Kara tried to staunch the bleeding with her hand. She tried hard not to acknowledge the warm oily feeling pouring through her fingers and spilling into the dirt. She knew it was ineffective. Futile. A word Lena had taught her. 

 

“H-hey,” Lena struggled to speak and flecks of blood flew from her mouth and dotted her chin. Tears were streaming down Kara’s face at this point. It felt like someone was hammering nails into her chest. She wanted to scream. 

 

“Hey,” Kara smiled down at her with watery eyes, “I’m gonna get you out of here okay? Just hold on Lena please.” Kara tried to move her away from the battlefield, but an anguished cry from Lena stopped her short. A gush of blood wet Kara’s already slick hand. She felt like she was going to vomit. She felt utterly powerless. 

 

“No- no, it’s too late for me. Don’t-,” Lena swallowed thickly, “Don’t worry about me. You need to get out- you need to survive… for me.” Lena raised a shaking hand to Kara’s face. Kara could feel the blood that smeared across her cheek and only wished she had more time. 

 

“Don’t worry about you?! You’re the only thing I care about now! Everyone I love is dead, Lena! Please don’t leave me alone!” Fat ugly tears were rolling down her face and onto Lena’s. Her chest felt extremely tight, like her muscle fibers were trying to collapse her rib cage. Pain wracked her entire being. She would have preferred it had been physical pain. She would take a thousand knives to the back over this. 

 

“It’s okay. It’s okay. I’m okay…” Lena’s hand fell limply away from Kara and threw a small puff of dust into the air as it landed in the dirt beside her. Her heart shattered. Kara felt like her soul was breaking into a million pieces. Her ears rang. She thought she heard someone screaming, but there was no one else around her. Not even the smoke or the fires stuck around with her. She only figured out she was the one screaming when her throat became painfully tight and her voice began to crack. Kara felt like someone had hollowed out her chest with a shovel and a rake. Her whole being felt raw and horrible. For a brief moment she considered giving in to the overwhelming weight on her soul that told her to just lie down and quit. 

 

“Don’t you want to find who did this?” 

 

“What?” Kara asked the empty air around her, clearly having heard a voice. It sounded like Lena’s voice. 

 

“Don’t you want to stop them from doing this to someone else?” 

 

“Yes! But Lena I can’t-“ 

 

“Don’t make my sacrifice be in vain. Don’t make your family and friends’ sacrifice be in vain. You can stop this from happening again.” 

 

“How am I supposed to go on without you? I feel like I’m ripped in half.” Kara felt crazy talking to herself like this. But she didn’t care. It didn’t matter. 

 

“I am always with you. You are a tattoo of infinite love across my heart, as I am to you. You will always carry my love, my strength, with you. And years from now, when it is time for your old body to take its final rest, I will be waiting for you.” 

 

Kara didn’t know who was talking to her. Was it really her? Lena? Or was it a fractured part of her consciousness as a result of the instant trauma she had experienced upon entering this field? She didn’t know. It didn’t matter. A fire had sparked deep in her gut and had been slowly breathing life into itself while Kara had listened to Lena. It burned itself into a roaring inferno that lit Kara’s entire being with a white hot flame that filled her mind with a single intent: find whoever was the cause and stop them from ever doing this ever again. By whatever means necessary. 

 

Kara gently laid Lena’s body, which felt so small and fragile to her now when it had only ever felt strong before, down in the dirt. She carefully crossed her arms over her chest and laid her torn cloak over her. With the wound covered, Kara could almost imagine she was sleeping. Her already pale face was now ghostly and grey, almost like a beautiful statue. The cherry red flecks of blood glinted harshly on her chin in the firelight of the battlefield around them. With Kara’s enhanced eyesight, she could see her own devastated reflection staring back at her in each shiny fleck. She knelt down and pressed one last anguished kiss goodbye to Lena’s cooling forehead and stood back up. One last tear rolled off Kara’s chin and spattered Lena’s cheek. It almost looked like she was crying too. 

 

She turned and began searching around her for any significant sign of who her enemy may be, but instead found the battlefield dissolving gently around her. She was frightened at first, but as the ground melted away under her feet and took all the horrible sights she had seen with it, a steady sense of calm and comfort washed over her. She was surrounded in pitch black nothingness, floating motionlessly through space. The raw ache still sat immovably in her chest, and though she knew to some capacity that Lena was still with her on Terra’s island, Kara would never be able to forget the way Lena’s eyes had looked when the light of her magic finally went out. 

 

She was brought back into the present gently, and found herself laying on the ground curled into herself. Her hand was still clasped in Lena’s. She could feel tears streaming down her face, falling onto the crystal plateau beneath her. She could hear Lena’s trembling breath beside her and the hitch in her chest as she cried quietly. 

 

“You’re back in the real world, my dears. You’ve passed the judgement of fortitude. I’m sorry you’ve had to endure so much suffering.” Terra knelt beside the two of them and pressed a soothing palm to the center of their backs. Dark green magic light spilled out of her hands over their bodies and filled them with a sense of gentle euphoria. Kara’s muscles relaxed, and Lena’s breathing evened out slowly. Soon enough, both of them had stopped their crying. Kara rolled over onto her back to face the darkening sky above them, her hand never leaving Lena’s grasp. They must have been under Terra’s spell for much longer than she had thought, the pain she felt in the vision had made telling time difficult. 

 

Lena soon followed suit and rolled onto her back, letting a shaky breath rattle out of her chest as she relaxed onto the crystal. She stared up at the sky, uncharacteristically clear that night and tried to busy her mind counting the first evening stars that had begun showing through the setting sun. She never wanted to think about that trial again. She never wanted to see Kara like that again. It was awful. Soul crushing. Heart breaking. She didn’t think that she would be able to find the will to stand back up after watching the life die from Kara’s face. It was an image she would never forget, and she felt the mental scar deep in her psyche. Kara’s warm hand clenched in hers was the only grounding root to this world. She feared that if she let go of Kara’s hand that she would never get to hold it again. She tried her best to put all these unpleasant memories and thoughts out of her mind, but they stuck around like an itch she couldn’t quite reach. 

 

Terra sat on the ground in front of them and waited patiently for them to be ready to sit up and move on. Terra felt bad knowing the magnitude of what they had just been through and wished that the trial didn’t have to be so harsh. 

 

“When you two are ready, you may decide whether you want to rest here for the night or not. There is food aplenty here. Rest and replenish.” Her voice was gentle and sweet, and Kara could see why Selene liked Terra so much and why it would hurt so badly to have that relationship threatened. 

 

“We should stay the night and rest. I’m sure Onyx will be happy to stretch his legs off of the boat.” Lena’s voice rang out from beside Kara. She sounded tired and absolutely drained. Kara couldn’t blame her. She felt the same way. All she wanted to do was wrap Lena in a hug in the safety of a warm bed on dry land and never let her go. 

 

“I agree. A night of rest will do us good.” Kara squeezed Lena’s hand, and Lena smiled at her in return. The two sat up, finally letting go of the other’s hand. The world was growing dark, and the last orange rays of the sun were reflecting beautifully off of the surface of the crystal on the mountain. Terra smiled at them and patted the ground gently. Slowly, a banquet table made out of the same crystal began to rise out of the ground. 

 

“I will gather food for you here. Taurine can escort you back down the mountain to gather your things and bring you back up.”

 

“Is Taurine the little cairn that helped us get here?” Kara asked. 

 

“Yes! He’s a cutie, no?” Terra smiled and began to walk down the trail on the spine of the mountain. On her fifth step down, she began to melt into the ground, becoming one with the mottled rock as she made her way down the mountain until she disappeared completely into the spine. 

 

“Woah,” Kara murmured. The wind was knocked out of her lungs by a sudden crushing hug from Lena from behind. Kara could feel her shaking as she cried into her back. Kara quickly turned around and dropped to her knees to bury her head into Lena’s chest. Their red and blue energies came crashing together frantically, as if they couldn’t be back together quickly enough. Kara hugged Lena as tight as she dared without hurting her and sobbed into her shoulder as she remembered her trial. Neither one of them spoke for a long while and just cried into the other. Kara couldn’t tell whether her tears were from sadness or relief at the fact that it wasn’t real and that she hadn’t actually lost Lena. Kara tried her best to memorize everything about the way Lena felt, smelled, sounded, looked. Everything. 

 

“Please don’t ever leave me here,” Lena choked out through sobs as she stroked Kara’s hair. 

 

“Never. Never. I’ll never leave you.” Kara took a deep calming breath and basked in the sweet scent that she had come to associate only with Lena. 

 

Kara’s ears pricked up as she heard the tiny steps of Taurine clacking toward them on the crystal. The two paused their embrace to look up at him. He waved one of his stubby little hands, urging them to follow him, and began walking back down the spine of the mountain to the platform they’d seen before. They followed closely behind, hand in hand, never without contact between them. His stubby legs carried him slowly in front of them, and they had no problem catching back up to him. He led them to the same platform and sat down to perform his magic. Light spiraled out from his hands, and they stepped onto the platform behind him. The platform carried them down into the heart of the mountain and swiftly moved past the open chamber full of gems that they had passed through before, falling deeper and deeper into the base of the mountain. At last, they were brought to a small cave where their slab finally landed with a dull thud on the stone. Light glowed into the tunnel from an entrance about twenty yards away from them. Taurine made no move to get off the platform, but looked up at them expectantly. 

 

They stepped off the slab, and Taurine waited for them quietly, motionlessly. They walked away from him and toward the weak light of day shining into the cave. The two emerged from a nearly hidden entrance about three hundred feet from where the giant hand had set their boat. Kara marked the entrance in her memory and led Lena to the boat to gather up just a few supplies. Kara leapt and heaved herself up onto the boat then held her hand down to help Lena as well. They grabbed only a few blankets and some water canteens to fill. 

 

Onyx was more or less happy to see them and followed them indifferently off of the boat when they beckoned him. He jumped easily from the railing of the boat into the sand. He meowed discontentedly at the feeling of the sand between his toes and trekked across the beach unhappily. They again reached the cave entrance where Taurine was still waiting for them in the back with the platform. Onyx sniffed at his head curiously and gave it a cautious lick. He nearly knocked Taurine’s head off completely, so he shooed Onyx away while he reoriented his skull. Onyx merped quietly like an apology and sat back on the back corner of the platform. Taurine used his magic to take them swiftly back to the top of the mountain and again shooed them off at the exit. 

 

The three watched him disappear back into the mountain and made the rest of the trek to the crystal plateau. They found Terra there waiting for them seated with an abundance of fruits and vegetables from around the island at the table she’d made. Kara’s mouth watered at the thought of food that wasn’t rice, beans, or fish. The colorful array looked beautiful as the sun set over the ocean around them. Since the sky was so clear, Kara wondered if they would see Selene’s moons in the sky tonight. There were already hundreds of stars battling against the weak sunlight to be seen, and Kara could see many more beginning to peek through as the sun set. Lena and Kara dropped their blankets and took their seats together opposite Terra. Onyx took himself underneath the table and grumbled unhappily. 

 

“Young one,” Terra addressed Onyx under the table, “There are many things for you to hunt on the island. You have my blessing.” 

 

Onyx not only understood her, but jumped up from under the table enthusiastically and ran back toward the spine of the mountain. His claws clacked loudly against the ground as he ran, and Kara felt sorry for whatever he chose as his prey that night. 

 

“How do you all know about Onyx?” Lena asked. 

 

“It is one of the markers that Ion told us about you. That and your names.” Terra sounded like she was choosing her words carefully. 

 

“How does Ion know about us? Why are they expecting us?” Lena pressed further. 

 

“I am not allowed to say any more. Ion has made it clear that he is going to explain everything when you reach him. Astraeus will not be able to tell you any more either.” Terra was firm in her words, but sounded like she wished she could help more.  

 

“How long is the journey from here to reach Ion?” Kara asked her. 

 

“It is about another month of sailing, but Astraeus and I will be able to help you travel more quickly. The distance between my island and Selene’s is about a week long boat ride, but she expedited the process for you.” She gestured to the uneaten meal in front of them. “Go ahead, help yourselves. Replenish. I know the judgement is a hard process even after it is over. I wish it didn't have to be so.” She picked up a bright red fruit and sank her teeth into it with a wet crunch. Amber juice spilled out of the fruit and rolled down the skin of it. Kara’s mouth watered, and she grabbed her own fruit with bright green skin. 

 

“If you’d like,” Terra said around a mouthful of fruit, “I can perform a rejuvenation spell in the pool of purity here.” She pointed to the pool that Kara and Lena had found her in earlier. They both nodded eagerly, and Terra smiled at their confirmation. “After dinner then.” 

 

The three of them ate in a comfortable atmosphere of quiet conversation. Terra asked them questions about their homes and their lives before they met each other. It seemed like such a long time ago to Lena and Kara since they had met, almost as if they had always been together. They’d spent every waking moment together for a month and had not grown tired of each other’s presence once. They found out that Terra and Selene had been quarreling for two hundred thirty-three years and six months, and that Terra had kept track of every day avidly. Kara and Lena offered their best advice to Terra; they hoped that she and Selene would stop arguing soon, because Terra was a very sweet lady, and it made Kara feel bad for her that they were fighting. 

 

The last rays of the sun faded quickly, and the three were very soon bathed in the blackness of the night. Selene’s four moons peeked over the horizon and cast their milky light on Terra’s island. Kara could tell Terra had no ill will against Selene; it was written all over her face in the way she looked up to the moons. She felt like it was the same way that she looked at Lena. Now that she knew exactly how it felt to lose her, Kara wouldn’t wish that on anyone else. The chattering of the animals in the trees quieted to a low hum as the night progressed. Onyx came back a while later with a noticeably full-looking belly and flopped down heavily onto the ground, not even bothering to wait for any blankets to be set out for him. Small snores began to rattle out of his chest moments later. Terra smiled at Onyx fondly. 

 

Lena thought it was nice to feel like she had made a new friend in Terra. She was very generous and sweet, and very understanding of their struggles. She offered to show Lena a thing or two about real earth magic, much more effective than the spells in any of her old books. Lena couldn’t be sure, but she thought that Terra might have also been using a weaker form of the calming magic that she’d used for them earlier after her trial on them throughout the evening. It felt like she was just the slightest bit wine drunk, but she still had her clarity. Lena thought it was a very kind gesture and that it showed Terra was a very empathetic and thoughtful being. She hoped her situation got sorted out soon with Selene. Lena thought that Terra’s expression when she talked about Selene reminded her of Kara when she caught her staring absently, and she couldn’t bear the thought of her being so unhappy. 

 

The night marched on, and soon Lena and Kara were both muffling yawns throughout conversation, so Terra finally suggested that they bathe in the pool before they laid down to rest. She stood and guided them to the edge of the crystal that the pool was carved from. When Kara looked over the edge she couldn’t tell how deep it was because the water was so clear, but it seemed incredibly deep. Kara and Lena paused, unsure if Terra was going to turn or leave. 

 

“Are you two seriously worried about being naked? It’s only your most natural state of being.” She smiled at these silly humans so bound by societal constructs. 

 

“I mean- yes, but…” Lena trailed off. 

 

“But…? What? You’re worried I’ll see something that I haven’t before?” She smiled, clearly amused and holding back laughter. Lena’s face reddened and she looked ready to protest until Kara began undoing the buttons on the collar of her tunic and pulled it easily over the top of her head, baring her chest. Lena caught herself compulsively staring at Kara as she continued to strip and only broke herself free of her reverie when she heard Terra’s light chuckle at her. Lena blushed in embarrassment and quickly began to follow suit after Kara. 

 

Soon they were both stripped down, and Lena crossed her arms uncomfortably over her chest and made intense eye contact with the ground. Kara’s hand made light contact with Lena’s back and ran gently over her scars. Goosebumps raised under Kara’s touch, and Lena couldn’t help but shiver slightly. Terra cleared her throat lightly. 

 

“You may enter the pool now.” Terra spoke softly. The gentle breeze carried her voice like a soft song through the air and helped to soothe the tension out of Lena’s shoulders. Lena and Kara toed the edge of the crystal at the water and Kara stepped in first. She sank easily into the water, her hands holding her to the edge. The water was just warm enough to be pleasant and felt somehow soft on her skin. She dipped her head beneath the surface and basked in the silence that resonated through the water. She broke the surface and wiped the water out of her eyes just in time to see Lena slip in after her. Lena tried to let herself relax and enjoy the feeling of the water on her sore muscles.

 

“I’ll begin the spell now. Remember that you are safe here.” Terra sat by the edge of the pool and dipped her legs into the water. She closed her eyes and began to hum quietly to herself. Her whole body began to glow with a dark green light, casting beautiful reflections across the water. 

 

“Let yourself relax into the water. Align yourself with its calm energy.” Terra’s voice was low, monotone, almost as if she was in a trance. 

 

Kara breathed out concentratedly and let herself float on her back in the water. Lena observed her relaxed form and followed suit. Their hands met between them, and Kara clasped Lena’s tightly in hers. 

 

“Imagine you are melting. Dissolve into the water. Dissipate into the energy of the island. Align yourself with the life here, and let yourself be healed.” The water itself began to glow a bright green and cast a beam into the night sky. 

 

Lena could feel all the pain of the past few weeks of travel and training melting out of her muscles. The only point of her that felt solid was her hand in Kara’s. She felt like her physical body was blending into the water and being washed away with light. Her body felt pure. Even the ghost of pain that always sat deep in the scars on her back was melted away and replaced with serenity. She let out a shaky breath and felt the heat of all her anxieties slip through her teeth and dissolve into the night air. 

 

“Now, if you will, allow your conscious mind to wander. Let it be free of your worldly troubles and dance to its content.” Terra began to glow more vibrantly, her aura of magic beginning to change colors between every shade of green Kara could ever think of. Terra cast a beautiful light show across the water and Lena wished she could fully appreciate it, but her mind wandered. 

 

She tried to let her mind wander as Terra had instructed. Pleasant images of sunny days sitting in the dappled shade of a tree with a good book flitted through her thoughts. The smell of the flowers that bloomed at the edge of the clearing floated around her. The rustling of the leaves on the trees that sheltered her home drifted through her ears. A beautiful, heartstopping laugh peeled like a bell through her mind, and her consciousness halted its wandering path. Lena knew that laugh. She’d know that laugh anywhere. A bright smile filled her mind’s eye. A smile she’d seen a thousand times a day for over a month. Eyes so blue they put the whole of the Expanse to shame. Kara. Just Kara. Lena’s mind found it’s peace in the joy of Kara’s face. Lena didn’t know how long she basked in the light of Kara’s smile, but it didn’t matter. A minute, a year, it wouldn’t have mattered. It wouldn’t ever be long enough for her. 

 

At last, Terra’s humming tapered off and faded to a gentle end as her light dimmed until it petered out entirely. Lena felt her energy receding gently back into her body from the pool. It was a strange feeling resolidifying from the water. Kara’s hand twitched in hers, and Lena was brought fully physically back into the present moment. She squeezed Kara’s hand gently, and Kara returned the gesture. 

 

“Take your time, my dears, exit the pool whenever you wish. I know it is not pleasant to shake off the cleansing and step back into the world,” Terra said softly as she retracted her feet from the water and stood into a stretch. 

 

Kara sighed and opened her eyes to the milky light of Selene’s four moons. She didn’t want to come back, but she couldn’t deny she was eager to see Lena again in real life and not just in a vision. Her body felt light as she righted herself in the pool and drifted toward the edge with Lena. They both emerged and stood bare in the warm night air. Lena’s shyness had faded enough that she didn’t feel uncomfortable anymore. Her body felt too light and relaxed for her to care. They drip dried until they weren’t completely sopping wet and pulled their clothes back on with some effort. Kara wrung out her hair as best she could, but it’s thickness prevented much of the water from falling. It quickly left a wet stain on the back of her tunic, but the night was mild so it didn’t matter to her. 

When she was redressed, Lena busied herself hastily making a blanket palette from the meager belongings that they’d grabbed from the boat. She was near delirious from exhaustion and was single-mindedly getting ready for bed. Her body was relaxed to the point that she knew if she stopped moving she would surely fall asleep. 

 

Kara watched her and thought of the vision of the ring again and crept back over to Terra who was cleaning up from their dinner. The table sank back into the ground, leftover produce and all, and the cracks sealed seamlessly behind it. Kara tapped Terra on the shoulder gently to get her attention and made sure that Lena wasn’t listening. 

 

“Can I ask you a question?” 

 

“Anything, go ahead.” Terra nodded.

 

“So don’t tell Lena I asked anything, but what kind of gems do you have on your island?” Kara scuffed her shoe in the dirt, trying to act nonchalant. A gleam of curiosity shone over Terra’s eyes. 

 

“There are hundreds of different kinds and thousands of variations for every kind. Why do you ask?” She whispered to Kara, keeping her glowing eyes on Lena to make sure she couldn’t hear. 

 

“Are there sapphires? The deep blue kind.” Kara pressed further, still withholding her explanation. 

 

“Yes, of course. Again, why are you asking?” Terra’s voice was more insistent this time, her curiosity bubbling over. 

 

“Lena and I… we shared a vision. In the vision I proposed to her. I gave her a silver band with a dark blue stone on it. It was the first thing that I thought of when we first arrived and I saw all the gems here.” Kara’s face reddened as she explained her motives sheepishly to the goddess. A wide grin split Terra’s pretty face from ear to ear. 

 

“Are you looking for a stone for the same purpose?!” Terra could barely maintain the quiet volume of her voice over her growing excitement.

 

“I… yes. I’d like to be able to give her a beautiful ring like that someday. The gems on your island are gorgeous. I think that I could find one to make a gorgeous ring for her here.” Kara muttered quietly, trying desperately to contain the part of her that wanted to squeal and gossip about this part of her life. She’d never had a real friend besides her sister, and it was exciting to talk about her love for Lena with someone new. She smiled down at the ground and tried to keep Lena from seeing her grin. Kara saw Lena flop down on the palette and close her eyes, exhaustion winning over her iron will. 

 

“Quickly, while she’s asleep. I’ll take you to the center where the rare ones grow.” Terra grabbed her hand and ushered her quickly down the spine of the mountain. Terra held out her free hand, and the panel that hid the passage to the center flipped easily out of the way. Terra pulled Kara onto the platform behind her, and it fell much more swiftly than it had before, at almost a terrifying rate, into the center of the mountain. Terra stepped off of the platform, dragging a disheveled Kara along behind her, and bright spirals of light snaked out from her feet that illuminated the large cave with a soft amber glow. She led Kara to a broad swath of dark blue glittering gems lining a section of the cave wall. Kara stood in awe of their beauty for a moment before eyeing each one critically, looking for the perfect size and shine for Lena’s ring. Eventually, her eyes landed on a small stone, no bigger than a quarter, that would be just big enough to make two rings. Its color was a deep aquamarine that glittered beautifully in the soft light. Upon closer inspection, Kara could see tiny specs of a different mineral inside that were glowing a faint minty green. It looked like a tiny galaxy how the specs were arranged, and a strong pull in her chest told Kara that this was the one she wanted. 

 

“That one. There.” Kara pointed to the stone that she’d chosen. Terra grinned giddily. 

 

“Oh that one is one of my favorites. I’ve had my eye on it for a while. None of the other gems here have the same mineral imperfection. It’s particles of a different stone inside that are glowing. This is a wonderful choice, Kara. I’m sure she’s going to love it.” Terra’s fingers glowed slightly as she plucked the stone in its entirety from the wall. With the gem’s base pulled free from the wall, its size was about the length of Kara’s ring finger and just slightly wider. Its glowing particles glittered as Terra placed it gently into Kara’s waiting hand. Kara took a moment longer to admire its beauty before she threw her arms around Terra’s neck in a friendly hug. Terra seemed a bit shocked and patted Kara’s back with one hand. 

 

“Thank you. So much. This means everything to me.” Kara broke her hug and smiled brightly at Terra. 

 

“You’re welcome,” She scratched the back of her head, “I consider you a friend now, so I better hear all the details when you pop the question!” She punched Kara lightly on the shoulder, and Kara was shocked when she felt actual substance behind its impact.  

 

“Of course, of course,” Kara smiled and dropped the stone into a drawstring bag that she triple knotted shut at her belt. 

 

“Let’s hurry back, before Lena wakes up and finds both of us missing,” Terra said and began walking back to the platform. Kara followed behind her, excited to find a way to carve her gem for Lena. The platform took them swiftly back to the spine of the mountain, and the two walked quietly into the clearing. Lena was still asleep, but Onyx was wide awake and staring at them suspiciously from the dirt that he’d fallen asleep in. Kara walked over to him and gave him a pat on his head. 

 

“It’s okay, Onyx. Go back to sleep.”

 

He took her at her word and closed his eyes readily. Terra whispered her parting words for the evening and headed back down the mountain, leaving Kara awake at the top. She sat down on the pile of blankets next to Lena and smiled down at her for a moment. Her face was peaceful and relaxed, so unlike earlier in the day after their trial when she had looked tired within an inch of her life. Kara stroked her hair softly, feeling the weight of the gemstone hanging on her belt, and her heart beat just a little faster. Lena sensed Kara’s presence in her sleep and rolled over to fit her head onto Kara’s lap. Her arms wrapped tightly around Kara’s thigh, and she sighed contentedly in her sleep. 

 

“I love you,” Kara whispered to Lena as she continued stroking her damp hair softly. She sat awake for a while longer, letting Lena hold her leg as she slept. Kara watched Selene’s four moons shift overhead as the night waned and the first gray light of the dawn peeked over the horizon. She hadn’t meant to stay awake for this long, but she hadn't had the heart to disturb Lena’s comfortable position. She also couldn’t quite manage to quiet her mind down. Her eyes felt heavy and she knew the lack of sleep was a bad idea at this point, but she didn’t care. She would sleep on the boat later. The last of Selene’s moons disappeared beneath the horizon as the sun cast its first gentle rays of light on Terra’s island. Within moments, all the animals began to chatter and screech intermittently in the trees, and Kara knew the world was waking up around her. 

 

Lena began to stir in her lap and stretched her sore legs out until they shook. She blinked the sleep out of her eyes slowly and stroked Kara’s thigh as she roused herself into wakefulness. Her eyes adjusted to the light, and she looked up at Kara adoringly. Like she was actually the sun lighting up her world. 

 

“Good morning darling,” Lena sat herself up and pressed a kiss to Kara’s lips, “How did you sleep?” 

 

“Good morning, Lee. I didn’t,” Kara chuckled lightly. 

 

“What?! All night? Why not, Kara?” Lena cupped her face, concerned. 

 

“Just couldn’t sleep. Plus you were comfortable in my lap. I didn’t want to disturb you. It’s not often that you sleep heavily through the whole night.” Kara placed her hand on top of Lena’s on her face. 

 

“You don’t have to lay awake at night for my sake, Kara, please.” She pleaded softly. 

 

“It wasn’t just because of that. I just wasn’t tired.” It was only a half truth. She had been just as exhausted as Lena had been, but the excitement boiling over in her chest about the gemstone sitting heavily in the pouch at her belt made sure sleep was nowhere near her mind. Even after her excitement boiled down to a level that would have let her sleep, she was reminded of Terra’s trial and the gut wrenching pain she’d experienced had set heavily back in her stomach. 

 

“You can sleep when we get back onto the boat. You can lay in my lap. I can read or something. What did you do all night?” Lena asked. She stroked her thumb gently across the healed scar on Kara’s chin. It had thinned out and become slightly more raised, but it wasn’t glaring. Lena thought it was quite handsome. 

 

“Looked at the stars and the moons… looked at you mostly,” Kara admitted with a sheepish blush. 

 

“You’re sweet,” Lena whispered and pressed a soft kiss to Kara’s lips again. 

 

“Good morning!” Terra’s loud bellowing voice rang out across the top of the mountain and startled them apart. “I thought I would treat you two to some fresh water and food before you continue the journey. After you pass Astraeus, Ion’s home has many bountiful resources.” 

 

“Thank you,” Kara yawned and stood up from their palette. She helped Lena to her feet and they made their way to Terra who was raising the same banquet table out of the ground. It came to a grumbling halt, and Kara quickly took her seat, exhaustion weighing on her body. 

 

“Where did the food that we didn’t eat from last night go?” Lena asked as she took her seat next to Kara. Her hand fell on Kara’s knee under the table and Kara welcomed the warm touch.  

 

“Reabsorbed into the earth. Life is cyclical.” Terra waved a glowing green hand, and many cairns much smaller than Taurine came waddling out of the forest with more fresh fruit for them. One by one, like ants to a nest, they dropped their hauls on top of the table and departed back into the forest without any trace. Kara’s tired gaze wandered absently after them and lost its focus for a moment at the edge of the crystal plateau. Lena tapped her shoulder and handed her a bright yellow round fruit with black dots covering the skin. It tasted light and fresh with just a hint of sweetness, and Kara was thankful for the refreshing feeling. Lena and Terra conversed quietly while Kara spaced in and out of the conversation as her tired mind pleased. Breakfast passed quickly, and Terra reabsorbed the table into the mountain while Lena and Kara gathered up the blankets that they had brought up with them. Kara filled the water canteens from the pool at Terra’s instruction while Terra and Lena talked about earth magic. 

 

“I wish I had more time to teach you, but the most important thing to remember is to keep your energy centers clear. For earth magic, specifically your sacral energy center.” Terra gestured to the space below her abdomen. “Your sacral energy center is the root of your connection to the earth and its magical frequency.” 

 

“Does the degree of effectiveness depend on how clear the energy center is?” Lena inquired. 

 

“Exactly. I know it is a hard concept to grasp and practice with no guidance, but I hope this advice will at least help to prepare your mind in advance. Ion is a master of his energy. He’s spent millennia meditating and training to keep himself centered. He’s admirable even among the Celestials.” Terra smiled as if she were talking about a good friend. 

 

“Is Ion a Celestial?” Lena asked. 

 

Terra pondered for a moment, considering what she was allowed to say per Ion’s instruction. She said, “No. But he is immensely powerful. He’ll be able to help you on your journey.” 

 

“Hm, I see. How many energy centers are there in the body? Is each one connected to a different element?” Lena fired off her questions. 

 

“There are seven, and not necessarily. There are elements that correspond more strongly with some energy centers’ magical wavelength, but not every energy center is connected to a natural element.” Terra seemed excited to share her knowledge with Lena. She would have shared more but Kara returned with the canteens and pointed to Onyx who was waiting impatiently near their palette. Lena and Terra cut their conversation short so Lena could gather up their blankets. 

 

Onyx waited impatiently at the top of the spine for them while they dawdled and spoke with Terra. Instead of sending them off with Taurine, Terra opted to guide them back down to the boat herself. She used the same platform to take them down through the heart of the mountain and walked them leisurely out to their boat. Onyx trotted through the sand impatiently, urging them to walk faster. She hugged both Kara and Lena tightly around the shoulders and wished them luck on their journey. 

 

“I’ll use my power to expedite your journey. The path from here to Astraeus is long, but I can make it about a day or two for you.” She smiled and patted Lena on the shoulder. 

 

“Thank you, Terra,” Kara said and then added more seriously, “For everything.” Terra grinned at their shared secret and gave them one last parting hug before stepping back. Instead of making Kara climb up first and pull Lena up behind her, Terra raised a solid square of stone out of the silky sand under their feet that lifted them up to the deck. Onyx yelped in surprise and leapt off the stone onto the deck as soon as it stopped moving. Kara and Lena stepped onto the deck and the rock slid back underneath the sand seamlessly. Terra waved her goodbyes, and a hand of sand, smaller than the mountain giant’s, but big enough to grab their ship easily, rose and gently pushed their boat off the shores of Terra’s island. 

 

Lena and Kara waved until Terra was just a small dot behind them and dropped their blankets onto the deck. Lena fell into Kara’s arms and wrapped her in a tight hug. Eventually they both fell to their knees, and Kara enveloped Lena with her body, shielding her from the world. Unbeknownst to them, Onyx rolled his eyes at their dramatics and began to drag blankets across the deck to the closed cabin door. Being alone, the weight of the trauma and devastating sadness that they’d both experienced hit them like a plow horse. 

 

Lena felt the sturdy plane of Kara’s chest underneath her fingers, felt the hard bone of her sternum that protected the beating heart inside her. Her mind flashed to the red stream that had poured from the same place in her vision and her hand fisted in Kara’s shirt. She breathed in Kara’s fresh scent and let the familiarity of it wash over her like a cool wave. She felt the heavy thumping of Kara’s heart thudding under her hand and heard her breathing next to her ear. She let herself be awash in all of the things that she would miss if Kara were ever taken from her. Her throat felt tight, and her chest laced itself with pain. 

 

“Kara?” Lena croaked out. 

 

“Yes, Lee?” Kara’s hand smoothed softly over Lena’s hair. 

 

“Please-,” she swallowed, “Please don’t sacrifice yourself for me.” 

 

“What do you mean, baby?” Kara’s voice was soft, gentle. 

 

“In my vision, you- you took a sword for me. Here.” Lena’s hand pressed hard against Kara’s chest, and hot tears began to slide from her eyes. “Blood, Kara, there was so much blood… I tried to stop it. I tried to heal you. It was too much. It felt hot on my hands. Slick like oil. I could feel your magic in it, pouring out of your chest and between my fingers. I could feel you dying before it happened.” Lena’s voice broke and she began to sob quietly into Kara’s chest as her tears soaked her shirt. 

 

“I’d-,” Kara swallowed thickly, “I’d like to say I could promise you that, Lee. I want to. I can’t though. I can’t-,” she choked back a sob, “I can’t watch you die again.” Lena’s arms wrapped tighter around her, and they both cried for a long while, cherishing the life each of them held in their arms. Kara felt the weight of the gemstone on her belt and felt even more certain of her decision. 

 

Terra’s island disappeared from their view, and the sun beat down overhead. The waters were calm today, and the rays of the sun reflecting off the surface were blinding. Eventually their crying softened and waned until it was just sniffles here and there while they breathed each other in. Kara relaxed into Lena and nearly fell asleep on the spot. She hadn’t realized that she’d been dozing until she startled awake when she began to slip to the side. Lena pulled back from their embrace and gave her a watery smile. 

 

“Are you tired, darling?” Lena smiled at Kara and wiped one last remaining tear from her cheek. 

 

Kara nodded her head sleepily and struggled to keep her eyes open and focused on Lena. Lena chuckled and helped Kara to her feet.
