“Let me do you,” Lena leaned up to capture Kara’s lips in a kiss. She loved the taste of herself on Kara’s tongue. 

 

“Not right now, Love. Later I promise I’ll get my turn. I just want to hold you close to me right now,” Kara kissed her tenderly. 

 

“I love you,” Lena whispered as she opened her eyes and broke their kiss. 

 

“As I love you,” Kara whispered back. Lena turned and snuggled herself back into Kara’s chest. She’d tried spooning Kara a few times, but Lena just felt like a backpack instead of a big spoon. Kara wiped her mouth on a corner of the sheet; they would need to be washed today anyway.

 

Onyx huffed loudly from the corner on his pile of blankets and stared discontentedly at the wall, pointedly avoiding eye contact with the two. He seemed bored, and his low energy drew Kara and Lena’s attention. 

 

“I wish we had something for him to play with. I hope he’s not too bored,” Kara said against the back of Lena’s neck. 

 

“Oh he can play any time we’d like, but he gets destructive as hell when he gets excited. I didn’t want to owe Lucy a whole new boat after this is all over.” Lena chuckled at the memory of the last time she’d let Onyx really let loose around the cottage. 

 

“Aww but he’s bored , Lee,” Kara whined, knowing the pet name would take Lena’s defense to at least half strength. 

 

“Do you want to explain the massive gouges and holes in the cabin to Lucy?” She asked. 

 

“Why? Are you going to explain the handprints in the railing and the crater in the deck to Lucy?” Kara countered and felt Lena’s defense crumble before her. 

 

“That’s fair. Honestly, I forgot about the handprints… I hope Lucy doesn’t notice them.” Lena said through a grimace. 

 

Kara laughed heartily at Lena’s discomfort, knowing it would be worlds more embarrassing for Lena to explain how they got there than it would be for her. She squeezed Lena tight and sat up in the bed. 

 

“How does he play?” She asked. 

 

“He doesn’t. Like I said, he destroys things. But… I suppose, since he’s so pitiful and you’re so cute, that we can make an exception.” Lena rolled her eyes and agreed begrudgingly. Lena sat up against Kara’s chest and began to rub her pointer finger and thumb together quickly. A small spark flew from her hand, and, eventually, a white glowing ball of light encapsulated the tip of Lena’s finger. She flicked her finger and the ball of light flew across the room and landed like a wet gumdrop on the floor. The blob perked up into a perfect sphere and began to dart randomly across the floor. 

 

Kara saw Onyx spring to attention, ears cut back and pupils blown wide. He bunched himself into a ball and locked onto the light with laser focus. Kara watched his muscles flex and pull under his fur and thanked every deity that may exist that she wasn’t his prey. Suddenly, his whole body went completely still. Kara saw one more minute twitch of muscle and then Onyx was a blur of razor sharp teeth and claws tearing across the cabin at an unbelievable speed. Kara could barely keep up with his motions as the ball skittered millimeters out of the reach of his claws at every turn. 

 

Lena yelped and pulled her feet up underneath herself, tucking her body into Kara’s to shield herself from any stray claws. Kara’s arms wrapped around her and they both scrambled to the top of the mattress against the wall and tried to keep all of their appendages from harm‘s way. Lena could hear the boards splintering under Onyx’s deadly claws and made a mental note to wear shoes in the cabin to avoid splinters later. Onyx yowled angrily as the ball evaded his grasp yet again, and he flung himself angrily across the bed in a flying leap to try to beat the ball to the other side. 

 

“Aah!” Lena screamed and hid as far underneath Kara as she could. Kara burst out laughing and hugged Lena underneath her body trying to shield her from the flurry of claws and fur that was flying around their room at breakneck speed. Kara laughed until she was wheezing and gasping for breath. Tears rolled down her cheeks, and Kara couldn’t think of a time when she’d found something this funny. Seeing someone as powerful as Lena reduced to a squealing cowering mess because of a particularly large and wily cat was comically exquisite. 

 

Kara hugged Lena securely into her chest and laughed heartily whenever Lena squealed in fear at Onyx. Eventually, Lena decided that the cabin had had enough and extinguished the ball just as Onyx’s paw fell on top of it. He lifted his foot cautiously to see if he caught it and was sorely disappointed to find that the ball had vanished entirely. He huffed angrily and curled himself on top of his blanket pile as if he hadn’t just destroyed most of the rest of the cabin, including several slashes in the sheets at the foot of the bed.

 

Lena eased her body gently from Kara’s and breathed heavily as her heart rate returned to normal. Kara’s arms remained loosely wrapped around her stomach which growled loudly in the silence that followed Onyx’s destruction. 

 

“I’m starving. I think I’ll go down and reheat whatever we have leftover for lunch,” Lena said as she ran a hand over Kara’s arm; a small habit she’d formed in the past weeks that Kara enjoyed immensely. 

 

“Alright, that sounds good. I think I’ll stay up here and journal a little bit before we eat.” Kara smiled and hugged Lena to her chest once more before releasing her from her embrace. 

 

“Sounds good, darling.” Lena crawled to the foot of the bed and carefully placed her feet on the splintered floor. She located her clothes and each of her shoes that had been flung from their original position at the foot of the bed in Onyx’s rampage. She slipped each of them over her bare feet and exited the cabin to head below deck. Kara watched after her until she left her line of sight, and Kara lingered for a moment, basking in the thought of Lena before pulling out her sketch pad and beginning an entry she intended to finish after lunch. 

 

Day 32

 

I know I can trust Lena not to look in my journal, and I’m glad because I have to get this out somewhere. This gem that Terra let me have is so heavy in my pocket, like it’s begging me to take it out and make something of it. I don’t have the facilities or the tools right now, but I want to. I want to so badly. Then again, Lena just told me she loves me. Even though I’ve known it for weeks now, it feels so much more real to me now that she says it in person. - Lena is calling me for lunch now I’ll finish later. 

 

Lunch was delicious as always. One of the things I love about Lena is that she’s able to make fish stew taste amazing for the umpteenth day in a row. I don’t know how she does it either, because Lucy doesn’t have hardly anything for spices on this boat. I feel bad about the splintered floorboards, but I also feel bad about the handprints and the hole in the deck. So I guess I just feel bad about Lucy’s boat in general, but a lot of it couldn’t be avoided. I still laugh a little every time I see the railing though. It always reminds me of Lena, and then I start thinking about how I never want to be without her. I hope our fates are intertwined for life. 

 

When we land I may ask this Ion if he has a place for me to work on Lena’s ring. If he lets us stay. I don’t understand what he is expecting us for. 

 

——-

 

Day 33 

Lena is still asleep. We trained after lunch today and I… really took it out of her. Anyway, besides that I got to spend a long time holding her and whispering lovely little things to her while she came down and I could tell by how her magic felt that she was so happy. I’m glad I can make her feel like that. I wouldn’t be planning on asking her to marry me one day if I thought I didn’t make her happy enough. I don’t know how it’s possible for me to love her any more than I already do, but every morning I wake up I’m wrong again. I’m okay with that though. 

 

I just hope we make it to the next landmark soon. I’m getting antsy being out on the open sea for so long. I love the solitude I get with Lena and how free we are to be ourselves out here, but I can’t help but miss the feeling of solid land under my feet. I want to be back in my element where I feel grounded and strong. I want to feel like I’m able to protect Lena. I don’t want to feel helpless. 

———-

 

Day 34. The sky suddenly became dark this morning and the winds have been getting stronger and stronger all day. Lena and I are nervous to even be out on the deck in case the waves throw another monster on top of us. I’m hoping this storm passes quickly. Lena’s protection spell is much stronger now than it was when we first left, but it still makes us nervous. We played with Onyx again today, and he caught my calf with one of his nails by accident. I know it was an accident, because it would have been a gash instead of a deep scratch like it is. He felt bad and came to apologize though. I hope that we can land at Astraeus’ island soon. 

———

 

Kara woke when she toppled off the side of the bed as their boat tipped nearly on its side. Lena yelped in surprise as she thudded onto Kara’s chest. 

 

“Damn it,” Kara hissed and grabbed onto Lena’s waist with one hand and the foot of the bed which was affixed to the floor with the other. For a moment until the boat righted itself, they hung in the air while Onyx climbed the walls as they rolled round. He yowled angrily up at them as Kara clung to the ceiling. 

 

“No offense Onyx, but do you really think we’re happy up here?” Lena yelled back down to him. He cut his ears back and huffed at her as the boat spun back around, and Kara and Lena were laid gradually onto the ground. The two, used to the turbulence at this point, stumbled out to the deck while Onyx remained in the cabin. Around them, winds stronger than Kara had ever seen before twisted the water around them into a frothing whirlpool. Directly in front of them above the center of the whirlpool, a massive piece of land floated above the water. Its base tapered off to a point about fifty feet above the water. The underside of the mass of land was covered with sharp, twisted rocks with numerous caves and pockets scattered across it. The top of it was obscured by swirling black clouds. Bright arcs of lightning curled like fingers around the outside of the cyclone; thunder boomed across the Expanse which made Kara’s sensitive ears ring uncomfortably. 

 

“Do we seriously have to figure out how to get up there?” Kara groaned. The boat lurched again, and she caught Lena against her chest before she fell on her ass. 

 

“I think so.” Lena struggled back to her feet and helped Kara to hers. “We need to figure out how to make the ship stay in place while we go up. It’ll be no use to make it to the top if the boat isn’t still here when we get back.” Kara nodded her head and began to try to think of a solution. The closer they came to the point of the underside, Kara could tell more easily how low the very point of it dipped. It was only about forty feet from their deck to the tip. 

 

“I might be able to throw one of Lucy’s harpoons like a spear and see if I can create an anchor with the rope for us,” Kara suggested loudly over the storm, “If we repower the protective spell and tie off to the base, the boat may even make it out undamaged. Well, at least not more damaged.”

 

“Are you going to climb up there to tie us off? That’s pretty far up…” Lena trailed off worriedly.

 

“I’ll be okay, Lee. I used to climb the cliffs by the beach all the time. This one is a little more… difficult, but I’ve never been one to back down from a challenge. Besides, Terra says we have to be steadfast and courageous.” Lena couldn’t argue with her and began her work pouring more magic energy into the protection spell while Kara headed below deck to grab the rope and the harpoon. Kara had dabbled with spears, not wanting to sell any weapon that she didn’t have experience with, and this was not different in any extreme way. Kara tied a tight, elaborate knot down the length of the shaft so that her weight during her ascent would pull the knot tighter instead of pulling it off the end.

 

As she came up the stairs Kara could feel the magic radiating from Lena as she muttered her spell. It amazed Kara how much Lena’s magical ability had grown since they had been on the open sea. It seemed like every day Kara watched Lena break limits she never knew she could before. On top of her magical ability, her hand to hand combat skills had flourished immensely under Kara’s watchful eye and careful hand. Lena had even truly bested Kara once or twice in their sparring sessions. Lena never had time to gloat before Kara had their lips locked together, though. Kara shook off her thoughts as the boat canted to the side once more, and she slid down the deck toward the railing. She quickly slammed the blade of the harpoon into the deck and stopped her momentum abruptly. She caught Lena by the arm as she slid past her and the two hung suspended over the Expanse past the edge of the railing. 

 

“Your spell won’t keep us in the boat if we fall overboard, will it?” Kara asked above the roar of the ocean around them. 

 

“No. Unfortunately not,” Lena yelled back and clung to Kara’s arm as the boat leveled out once more and they were laid back into the deck. 

 

“Can you make the navigation spell take us directly under the point? I think that’ll be my best vantage.” Kara wound the very end of her rope tightly around her hand so it wouldn’t get lost if her throw went wide. 

 

Lena nodded. “I’ll do my best.” She kept her eyes open to help with her balance and began her incantation again. Kara hadn’t seen Lena perform her magic with her eyes open very many times. It always shocked Kara how brightly her green eyes glowed. Red light began to glow around the steering wheel, brighter than before, and the wheel spun and jerked as it worked the boat through the rough waters until it rocked unsteadily in the center of the whirlpool directly underneath the point of the rock. It whipped constantly back and forth as it tried to keep them steady. 

 

Kara gripped the harpoon tightly in her hand and waited for the right moment. For a split second, their boat leveled out in a small calm spot between the waves and Kara took her chance. She imagined all the power inside of her channeled into her muscles as she wound up for her throw. She envisioned an explosion of power propelling the shaft from her hand, and she was startled by the bright flash of blue light that erupted from her fingertips. The harpoon shot at an incredible speed into the base of the land above them, trailing a bright blue arc behind it, and sank almost halfway down the shaft into the rock.

 

“Holy shit,” Kara heard Lena say over the roar of the storm. 

 

“I don’t think we’re getting that harpoon back,” Kara joked as she jerked on the rope. Solid. More than solid enough to climb, even for Kara’s massive form. She climbed on top of the cabin and wrapped the remaining slack around the steering column with only slight difficulty as the boat canted from side to side. She tied a tight knot and hopped back down onto the deck with Lena. The rope pulled taut and went slack as the boat bobbed up and down. 

 

“This is going to be kinda tough with the boat jerking on the rope like this. Luckily it’s a short climb.” Kara made sure that her sword and her gem were still strapped securely to her belt and grabbed onto the rope. 

 

“Kara… I don’t know if I can do that. I don’t have climbing experience like you. I’ve never even climbed a tree before.” Although she was yelling over the wind, Lena’s voice was soft and fragile, laced with insecurity in every word. 

 

Kara smiled. “I’ll help you. Don’t worry. Hop on my back and hold on tight. I’ll do the rest.” Kara knelt down so that Lena could jump onto her back. Lena climbed onto Kara and wrapped her legs tightly around her waist. Kara laughed loudly and stood up suddenly. Lena gasped and nearly choked Kara from behind as she tightened her arms in surprise. 

 

“Kara!” 

 

“Sorry, I just forget how little you weigh sometimes. I think it’s funny.” Kara laughed again and hiked Lena up on her hips to fit her waist more snugly. She grabbed onto the rope and gave it a test tug, barely lifting them off the roof. The harpoon didn’t budge an inch despite the fact that the boat tugged and yanked on it incessantly in combination with their weight. 

 

“Easy,” Kara muttered and began to climb. It was interesting, climbing a rope that was being jerked on from one end, but not impossible. Kara admittedly could have climbed to the top with just her hands, as she’d practiced many times before, albeit on a much smaller scale, in the small athletics area she’d gradually built behind her shop back home. She had made a tall tripod of straight wooden beams and hung a thick rope at the apex. It was crude, but it had worked and paid off in this moment. Lena weighed virtually nothing compared to her heavy iron chains and weights that she frequently used while practicing back home. Kara opted to steady them on the rope with her feet as well, and they ascended the first ten feet incredibly quickly. Lena watched as Kara’s muscles flexed across her whole upper body while she climbed, and became entranced by the rhythmic waves rolling beneath her skin. It never failed to amaze Lena how strong Kara was. Lena assumed it had something to do with Kara’s magical ability, but she couldn’t be certain. She also loved how safe Kara’s strength made her feel. She felt that with their combined strength and magical ability they could be nearly unstoppable. 

 

Eventually, Kara pulled them through the magical barrier surrounding their boat, and she was nearly blown off into the sea below by the winds that wailed around them. Lena’s arms tightened around her neck, and Kara nearly had to remind Lena not to accidentally kill her. Her arms loosened a moment later, though, and Lena compensated by crushing Kara’s midsection with her legs. Kara wrapped the rope around her right foot and steadied the rope underneath with her left. Her foothold gave her the slightest bit more stability in the wind and she resumed her climb. Lena’s teeth chattered in her ear, and Kara wished she’d brought a pelt for her to wear. She reached the base of the pole and began to pull herself onto the rocky base. Up close, there were many more visible handholds, almost like jug handles, and Kara smiled smugly at the easy task ahead. 

 

Lena, unaware of Kara’s confidence at the task ahead, shook like a leaf clinging to a tree in a tornado. The innate fear of falling from a great height in combination with the fear of the unknown ahead of them gripped her chest with icy fingers. Frigid wind whipped her hair around her face, and she struggled to keep her breathing even and just focus on Kara. The heat that radiated from Kara’s body was Lena’s only comfort as they made their steady ascent. 

 

Kara climbed swiftly toward the top of the incline, eager to be away from the threat of the Expanse below her. When she was about halfway up, she began to hear strange screeching sounds in the wind. She couldn’t tell if it was just her imagination or if there was some new threat for her to deal with.

 

“Do you hear that?” Lena yelled over the roar of the air. 

 

“Yeah. What do you think it is?” Kara called back.

 

“I don't know. I thought it might be the air whistling in the holes in the mountain, but… that sounds like screeching- It sounds alive.” Kara could hear fear lace itself through Lena’s voice as she spoke. 

 

“Keep an eye out for me. I’ll get us to the top as fast as I can.” Kara picked up the pace as she continued her climb, but she couldn’t help the leaden feeling growing in her stomach as the shrieking noise became clearer and louder over the winds. Once, Kara thought she saw a flash of something red swirling in the storm around them, but chalked it up to her imagination. The ledge of one of the many caves in the rock came into view, and Kara almost let herself feel relief that she could rest for a moment. Lena kept her eyes peeled, trying her best to find the source of the noise that was growing ever closer. Lena turned her head swiftly as something bright caught her eye. She squinted through the storm, trying to get a better look, and found two bright red glowing eyes flying straight at them at a terrifying speed.

 

“Kara, watch out!” Lena screamed. 

 

Kara turned her head and saw the eyes just in time to swing them off to the side, dangling by one hand over the Expanse below. Their ship bobbed and jerked in the waves and looked so tiny beneath them. Whatever body the eyes were attached to slammed into the rock wall, drilling another hole into the side of the mountain, flinging bits of sharp stone at Kara’s face. Lena screamed as she was sprayed with debris and clung tightly to Kara’s back. They both felt the sharp shards of rock scatter cuts and scrapes on every inch of exposed skin around their clothes. 

 

“Hold on!” Kara yelled and resituated herself on the rock face. 

 

“What the hell was that?!” Lena’s nails gripped onto Kara’s shoulders and her legs crushed Kara’s waist. 

 

“I don’t know. Did you see those eyes?” Kara asked, panicked. 

 

“The eyes were the only thing I could see!” Lena exclaimed. 

 

“Well they had to be attached to something for them to plow a hole into the rock like that! Something big, too!” Kara yelled over the wind and the sound of the creature screeching. 

 

“Agreed- but how can we deal with a threat that we can’t even see?” Lena asked. 

 

“Can you use your magic? Maybe a spell could help reveal its location?” Kara suggested. 

 

“I can try, but I’ve never used a spell like that before. I don’t know if it will work.” 

 

“It’s better than nothing at this point.” Kara squinted her eyes into the wind and tried to locate whatever had attacked them. After a moment, her gaze locked onto the two glowing orbs floating about fifty feet away from them. Kara noted that, although whatever the eyes were attached to was directly exposed to the gusts of wind around the island, it seemed unaffected by the force of it. Kara didn’t have too much time to think about how strange that was before the eyes were darting back toward them. 

 

“Shit!” Kara yelled as she scrambled up the rock face in a show of athleticism that was impressive even for her, quickly swinging them and propelling them across the rock with marked skill. The invisible body slammed into the rock around them again and again as Kara narrowly dodged its strikes. Lena tried her best to concentrate her magic power, but every time she tried to visualize her bright red glow, the creature slammed into the mountain and sprayed her with tiny razor sharp pieces of rock. It made it nearly impossible to concentrate. Even when she was able to concentrate for just a moment, any amount of her magical energy she could build up was blown away by the strong winds around her. She couldn’t comprehend it. Nothing physical like the elements or any natural interference had ever had any sort of effect on her magic before. She wasn’t even aware that her magic energy was something that could be influenced by physical factors like the wind. She tried again and again, but her magic was blown away every time. 

 

“Kara! I can’t use my magic!” Lena cried frustratedly. 

 

“What?! Why?!” Kara tried to look at her over her shoulder. 

 

“I don’t know! I think it’s the wind. It feels like it’s blowing my magic away.” She gripped tighter onto Kara’s shoulders. 

 

“Shit- Incoming!” Kara pulled them straight upward and flung her hands up to new handholds about five feet above where they had been. The eyes slammed into the rock again, and this time, Kara barely saw the outline of the creature they were attached to as it was covered in the dust from the rock. It had a gnarled, vaguely human body, with large wings that seemed similar to a bat’s. Kara could see patches of feathers sprouting at random across its body and was thankful when the wind blew the dust away so that she could no longer see its grotesque figure.

 

“Whatever it is, it’s big with wings and fast! We have to get to safety now!” Kara cried. She resumed her fast pace, dodging the random blows that landed on the rock all around her. As she continued her frantic climb up the mountain, the only goal in her mind was to make it to the cave so that she could keep Lena safe. Kara’s hand gripped a hold only three feet from the edge of the cave, and a terrifyingly powerful invisible hand gripped her right ankle with crushing force. Kara yelped in surprise and nearly lost her grip. She tried yanking her ankle free, but the hand did not budge. In fact, it gripped tighter and Kara knew instantly that this creature could mangle her with little effort. 

 

“Lena! You have to go!” Kara cried.

 

“What?! I’m not leaving you here!” Lena argued. 

 

“Please! I can’t fight this thing while I’m worried about you falling!” 

 

Lena seemed to struggle internally for a moment before sighing and scrambling off of Kara’s back onto the jagged ground of the cave. Lena struggled, having no experience climbing anything before, but managed to pull herself up over the edge with a great deal of effort. Lena scrambled to her feet and crouched over the edge, drawing her knife from its sheath. 

 

Kara struggled below, kicking blindly where she estimated the creature’s head to be. She connected one solid blow and the monster shrieked and hissed, spewing bright aquamarine blood from where its nose must have been. Its hand loosened momentarily, but then clamped back down with a force so devastating that even Lena could hear the crunching and splintering of the bones above Kara’s ankle. Even louder though was the scream that wrenched its way out of Kara’s chest as she felt her mangled leg squish beneath the pressure of the fingers. She felt splinters of bone digging into the pulverized flesh of her leg, and she nearly lost consciousness.

 

“Kara!” Lena screamed. 

 

Kara’s world narrowed to white hot, blinding pain. Her stomach wrenched and she almost lost what little food she had in her. Pain radiated up through her leg and into her pelvis. She didn't even know if she could bend her leg at the knee. She could already feel blood pooling in her foot. The hand clenched again and Kara howled in pain. Her hands nearly came off the rock wall, and a cold sweat beaded at her brow. She held herself up and kicked once more with all her might at the spot she could see blood spilling from. Her boot connected solidly with a wet crunch as she shattered its nose, and the invisible thing cried out again before finally releasing her ankle to clutch at its face. The blood that poured from its face and onto its body revealed more of its form to Kara as it coated its front. She could see the bright blue beads dripping off of the patchy feathers that sprouted from its potbelly. Kara could see rage and pain in its burning red eyes and used her last ounce of remaining strength to haul herself up over the edge with Lena before she rose to a pained kneel and drew her sword. The eyes floated ten feet in front of them but seemed like it was struggling to find an angle to attack them from. It tried several times to fly straight at them as it had before, but was met with the cold steel of Lena’s knife parting its flesh and spattering hot blue blood across the stone. She slashed and stabbed skillfully, utilizing all of the skills she’d gained in her training with Kara. The more it attacked, the more of its form was revealed as it was gradually covered from head to toe in wounds that seeped blood across its invisible body. Its wings were barely narrow enough to fit into the cave and Lena could see that it was growing weary of being wounded in its attempts to kill them. Kara saw flecks of blood spray from its lips as it shrieked at them one last time before diving backwards out of their sight. 

 

Kara collapsed to the ground and shivered. The wind howled outside the entrance of the cave and swirled around her body, chilling her. A strange, numb feeling was settling in all over her body, and all she wanted to do was lie down. She could faintly hear Lena calling her name over the roar of the storm, but she couldn’t bring herself to lift her head. 

 

“Kara! Please, baby, come on. You can’t lie down right now. I need you to get up. You'll be okay, I promise,” Lena rattled off mostly to herself, trying to convince herself that Kara was going to be fine even though her skin was ashen grey and Lena could see the limp point that should be straight in Kara’s leg under her pants.

 

“I- gods-” Kara turned her body away from Lena and lost the contents of her stomach on the ground next to her. 

 

Lenas hands were soft on her back, gently encouraging Kara to face her. Kara wiped the bile from her mouth and tried weakly to sit up. Moving her leg at all sent lightning bolts of agony shooting through her body, and Kara didn’t know how she was supposed to continue. How could she continue their climb? Surely her arms would be strong enough on their own to carry the two of them, but the pain in her leg made her whole body feel weak. She tried to sit up, and the slight jostle to her ankle made Kara see stars. 

 

“Aah-!” Kara yelped as she felt a piece of bone click over another. 

 

“Kara, I'm so sorry-“ Lena began but Kara waved off her apology. 

 

“Not your fault- now how do we get up?” Her face was ashen and Lena could tell she was trying hard not to lose consciousness. 

 

“I- I don’t know. I don’t think I’m strong enough to climb the rest of the way.” She turned her gaze to the floor. 

 

“It’s okay. I can still pull us up,” Kara said although she felt unsure. 

 

“How? You can’t even stand let alone hold me on your back.” Lena crossed her arms. 

 

“If I can crawl to the edge and get outside, I can hang on to the wall and have you climb onto my back.” Kara desperately hoped it would work. She hoped she had enough strength left in her body.

 

She painstakingly crawled her way to the edge of the cave and took a careful look around for the monster from before. She could see no sign of it and began to try to swing her body onto the wall. Her ankle dangled limply and each movement was agony, but she hung on. Lena, looking extremely uncertain, climbed awkwardly onto Kara’s back. The two dangled in the wind above the open sea and Kara felt the weight of their bodies rapidly depleting what little strength she had left. She began to swing them from hand hold to hand hold, coming dangerously close to slipping only a few times. With each slip and near miss, Lena grew more and more nervous about their ascent. Her only comfort was the fact that she knew Kara would sooner sign herself over to the queen than let them fall. She wished she could be of more help. She wished she could even just climb up herself if only to take the extra weight off of Kara. The top of the land finally came into view and Kara quickened her pace as much as her remaining strength would allow. By the time she managed to pull them both up and over the edge, she was drenched in sweat. She panted in the dry grass that rustled around her and took a moment to relax. She turned her eyes upward to assess the area around her. The entire island was flat rolling waves of grass and Kara could see nothing but one lone tree on the entire expanse. It was hard to tell through her pain-blurred vision, but Kara thought that the air around the tree looked calm; its branches seemed to remain perfectly still in the gale around them. She began to sit up.

 

“How much do you want to bet that we have to go there?” Kara joked weakly as she tried to get her good foot under herself. She leaned nearly all of her weight on top of Lena as she stood. Lena tried to pretend that Kara didn’t almost crush her as flat as this island. She shouldered Kara’s weight quietly, unwilling to make so much as even a whisper of effort or complaint in light of Kara’s current state. She felt it was the least she could do. 

 

“I think you're right. Can you walk at all?” Lena asked.

 

“If you prop yourself under my shoulder like a crutch then I might be able to hobble. Otherwise I think I’ll have to crawl.” Kara grimaced as she hopped on her good foot, the jolt of movement sending arcing pain through her ankle.

 

“You’ll have to take it slow. No offense but I can’t hold up your full weight on my own,” Lena said.

 

Kara barked a pained laugh, knowing it was the fault of her massive stature. Kara silently thanked the gods that it was her who had gotten caught by the creature and not Lena. Kara couldn’t imagine what damage that thing could have done to Lena whose body was so much smaller and more fragile by comparison. Lena fitted herself snugly under Kara’s arm and tried her best to support her weight. They took one step, and Kara stumbled. Her instinct to catch her fall with her other foot caused her to completely topple to the ground, taking Lena with her.

 

“Shit- I’m sorry Lena, are you okay?” Kara scrambled off of Lena as quickly as she could through her pain. Lena felt blood trickle down her knee and tried to hide her pain. 

 

“My knee is scraped, but Kara- don’t worry about me right now. I’m fine. How do we get you across this field?” Lena asked as she dusted herself off. It was definitely more than a scrape on her knee, more like a gash, and it ached. She ignored it. 

 

“I don’t know. I- I’ve never been hurt like this before. I’ve even broken my other leg before. But that thing- it completely crushed my ankle and everything above it. It won't even stay facing the same direction on its own.” Kara nearly gagged at her own words and slammed her eyes shut as the world began to spin around her. 

“Fuck- Kara I’m so sorry. How can I help?” Lena sounded near to tears, and Kara opened her eyes again to meet her gaze. 

 

“Hey,” she said as she raised her hand to cup Lena’s cheek, “I’ll be alright. I promise. We’ll splint it when we get back to the boat, yeah?” 

 

Lena nodded and smiled weakly before helping Kara back to her feet. They figured out it was best for Lena to remain still while Kara pushed off of her before moving ahead. It was slow progress, but steady at the least. Kara was sweating with the effort it was taking to stay conscious, but she managed. The closer they got to the tree, the more apparent it was that the air around it was calm for nearly a thirty foot radius. The clouds above it formed a swirling hole where the sunlight of the day streamed in brightly. The leaves of the tree were dark, almost black, but had a shimmer to them almost like an oil slick that made part of the leaves shine with a dull rainbow aura. Its white bark was nearly completely smooth, with only a few cracks here and there. Kara picked up her pace slightly, wanting more than anything to collapse into the soft grass in the still air beneath the tree. As they came within a few feet of the barrier that seemed to separate the tree from the storm, a tall figure emerged from behind the tree into their line of sight. 

 

Lena almost couldn’t believe what she was seeing. A very pale, handsome man, about seven feet in height with a fairly average build otherwise stood still in front of them. His chest was bare and he wore a black skirt around his waist. It was trimmed with shiny filigree similar to the glint on the leaves of the tree, blending into the fabric until the light struck it in a certain way. The closer Lena drew, she saw that his skin was so pale that he was nearly completely translucent, and she could see his entire skeleton through his skin when the light hit him between the shade of the leaves on the tree. Black reed-like structures protruded everywhere from his pale skin like spines, seemingly attached to his bones. They seemed to be cut at assorted lengths, some short and some long, and many in between. He made no further move to come any closer to them and waited. They broke the barrier of the storm and Kara collapsed at the edge of the circle, face first into the grass and dirt. The dirt was sandy and warm against her skin, and felt like a hug in comparison to the winds that wailed behind her. The grass was sparse but soft and green. She didn’t understand why the winds stopped where they did, but she didn’t care. The sunlight felt pleasant on her back and she was able to relax enough to dull the sharp throbbing pain in her leg to a dull ache. 

 

“Kara- are you okay, Love?” Lena asked. She crouched down and tried to ignore the parts of her brain telling her that she could feel the gash on her knee separating wetly with every move. The sunlight and the still air made it easier.  

 

“Mm,” Kara cracked a smile. 

 

Lena looked up at the man who had not moved since they first arrived. He stood like a statue and regarded them with an expression of complete neutrality. He didn’t even seem like he cared that they were here at all. 

 

“E-excuse me?” Lena called out, “Are you Astraeus?”

 

“I am.” His lips did not move, nor did any part of his body, but Lena knew that she had heard him speak. His voice was airy, like a flute, and ancient. 

 

“Uh- sorry, but did you say that?” She asked him.

 

“I did.” His lips didn’t move, but Lena noticed a slight breeze in their small still circle when he spoke.

 

“Pardon me if this is rude, but how are you talking?” Kara looked up at Lena’s words and seemed to see him for the first time. She seemed slightly taken aback, but more curious than startled. 

 

“I speak with the reeds.” The breeze swirled around him as he spoke, stirring the thick leaves on the tree gently. Lena wondered about the specific mechanics of his vocalization, but assumed it had something to do with the wind blowing across the open ends of the reeds, creating a whistling voice. She supposed now was not the time for specific questions, though, since he seemed only barely willing to answer her questions to the bare minimum anyway.

 

“Can you help us?” Lena asked.

 

“I can send you on your way. Nothing more. Ventus tells me that you are a capable enough pair.” He said. At their confusion he added, “He is my assistant that you met earlier. He said you gave him quite the beating, and it didn’t look like he was exaggerating.” 

 

“Capable? That thing crushed her leg! She can’t even stand!” Lena felt outraged. Now that the wind was calm around her, she could feel her magic growing into a swirling stormcloud.

 

“It is a trial, no?” He asked flatly. She couldn’t argue, but by comparison his trial had been much more deadly. The wind blew lightly, and he spoke again, “I am not akin to Terra and Selene. I intended to test you to the full extent of your ability, and I have done just that. I do not pity you. Or anyone. I’m not helping Ventus either; he is capable of licking his own wounds. I have seen stars born and die in my time on this planet. And even I am inconsequential in the grand scheme of the universe. To care is to stray from the true path of neutrality.” His posture never changed. His voice remained even and consistent. 

 

“Why couldn’t I use my magic? If it was a test of ability, why not let me use mine?” Lena asked. 

 

“You must adapt. I took your magic; Ventus took her strength. How you pass the trial from there is up to you. She chose to send you ahead. You chose to go. You both chose to stay and finish the fight to come here. It is as simple as that. The only thing you must do is adapt, and you have. The means do not matter. Nothing does, actually,” He spoke flatly. 

 

“So you don’t care about anything?” Kara asked incredulously. 

 

“No. Not since I was young, many millennia ago,” He paused, “I cared for a mortal. She lived, and passed on as they do, and I lived on as Celestials do. My pain nor my love meant anything substantial to the universe,” he said. “If something so seemingly substantial meant nothing, then nothing does.”

 

“Do you remember her name?” Kara asked and pushed herself into an uncomfortable sitting position in the sandy dirt. There was a long pause before he answered.

 

“Excuse me?” For once his whistling voice held the barest hint of emotion. 

 

“Her name. The woman you loved. Do you remember her name?” Kara asked more insistently. He seemed to pause for a long moment, regarding Kara with an unreadable expression.

 

Finally, he said, “Yes. Her name… it was Serenity .” When he spoke her name, all of his reeds hummed in a beautiful harmony. It was a lovely soothing sound that warmed Lena’s heart. 

 

“I can tell by the way you say her name. She meant something to you.” Kara smiled. “And I bet you meant everything to her, too. Why shouldn’t the way you felt about each other be significant, even if the universe didn’t care about your pain? You still love her. After so many millennia, that has to matter at least a little. Your pain has to matter at least a little,” Kara said and then added more softly, nodding to Lena, “Think of how she feels seeing me this way.” She didn’t know where these words were coming from, but they felt effective. “Think of how she would feel if she were to see you like I am now.” His slack face finally tensed as his brows furrowed tightly over his dark eyes as he tried to come up with an argument. He seemed to give in and breathed out a deep whistling sigh. 

 

“I will… assist you. Minimally.” He lifted his hand and cast a bright white light out from his fingertips. The light snaked out and curled softly like a mist around Kara’s destroyed ankle. The light was cool and soothing against her skin, and the pain in her leg instantly abated by a huge measure. It wasn’t completely gone, but it was worlds more bearable. It felt more like bad growing pains rather than broken bones now. Kara breathed out a relieved sigh and sank gratefully back into the dirt. 

 

“I have removed most of the pain, but I will do no healing. That is yours to do in its entirety.” He spoke softly and came closer to them. He knelt next to them and Lena could see that his eyes were dark, hollow spaces occupied by a thick shadowy mist. Not even eyes at all. Lena huddled closer to Kara’s side. He raised his hands and pressed his pointer and middle fingers to their foreheads. His hands were cold and felt lifeless against their warm flesh. He spoke once more, “I will return you to your vessel. Good luck. I hear Ion has many plans for you.” A white light glowed at his fingertips and chilled their bodies as they dissolved into the wind. 

 

Kara gasped as she rematerialized on the deck of the boat with Lena. Lena’s hand lurched out to grasp Kara’s hand. Their harpoon became loose suddenly, and clattered back onto the deck a safe distance from either one of them, dragging the rope behind it seconds after, which piled almost neatly on itself with a dull thud. Their boat sped through the worst of the storm spontaneously and spat them out into calmer waters on the other side of Astraeus’ island. Lena rose to her knees, gathering Kara into her arms and cradled her head against her chest. Kara relaxed into Lena and heaved a sigh of relief. Lenas knee throbbed and exhaustion weighed on every muscle in her body.

 

“Are you alright darling? I mean- besides the obvious.” Lena stroked her hand soothingly over Kara’s hair. 

 

“I’m alright now. I mean, I still can’t get up and my leg hurts a little, but I’m alright.” Kara tried to focus more on the feeling of Lenas fingers running through her hair instead of the pain radiating through her ankle. Kara couldn’t bear a glance at her wounded leg. She could feel more clearly now with the pain reduced that the creature had crushed all of the bones surrounding the joint of her ankle, as well as the ends of the two bones in her lower leg. It was a horrendous break, and Kara didn’t know how it was going to heal correctly. 

 

“We need to get your ankle stabilized. I can wrap it in a loose cloth and put a permafrost spell on that as well to help the swelling,” Lena said, eyeing the twisted joint. 

 

“This is going to hurt so bad isn’t it?” Kara asked sadly.

 

“I’m sorry, baby,” Lena responded with a kiss pressed to the side of Kara’s temple.

 

Lena helped Kara struggle into the bedroom where Onyx was waiting for them. He glared at them but actually seemed minimally concerned at Kara’s injured state. His blanket pile was rumpled and in no state for him to be sleeping. 

 

“I’ll fix it in a minute, Onyx, hold on.” Lena helped Kara fall gracelessly onto the bed and winced at Kara’s yelp of pain as her leg was jostled. Lena helped Kara settle in and then ran below deck to grab tools to splint Kara’s ankle. Lena found a wash rag that she planned to use for her frost spell, as well as a long bandage to wrap supports around her leg. She struggled finding anything that was straight enough and sturdy enough to stabilize the break, so she settled for breaking a broom handle over her knee to create two twin pieces. She got rid of the head of the broom and ran back to Kara with her supplies. Kara smiled at her arrival, but was clearly still in great pain. 

Lena quickly cast a weaker version of her permafrost spell over the cloth and wrapped it loosely around Kara’s ankle. Kara hissed in pain at the contact, but the cold was soothing. It was cold enough that it would help reduce the swelling, but not so cold that it would damage the tissue.

 

“Sorry,” Lena whispered as she worked. She positioned her splints and began wrapping Kara’s leg snugly. Lena heard one of the fragments of bone click as her ankle was repositioned and Kara gasped in pain. Her face went white and it was a good thing she was already laying down because she came within millimeters of passing out. Her head lolled to the side and her eyes rolled back as she struggled to maintain consciousness. Lena finished quickly and removed her hands from Kara’s leg as soon as she could. It reminded Lena of the first time she’d tended to Kara’s Firethorn burns at her little hut all those weeks ago. She wished things still felt as simple, but she wouldn’t change anything about their time together. Kara regained her full consciousness after a few moments. Lena crawled up onto the bed next to Kara, careful not to jostle her leg in the slightest, and tucked herself into Kara’s side. 

 

“I’m sorry I couldn’t help you more today,” Lena murmured. 

 

“What do you mean?” Kara asked.

 

“If I could have used my magic, or if I was stronger, I could have helped you. You wouldn’t be so hurt.” 

 

“Astraeus said that he took your magic. It probably had something to do with the wind around the island; you said you felt like your magic was being blown away.” 

 

“If I were stronger-” 

 

“You're the strongest person I know, Lee. Don’t tell my sister I said that or she’ll kick my ass.” Lena smiled and gave Kara a gentle kiss.

 

“What do you want to do now?” Lena asked.

 

“I kind of want to bathe. I feel sweaty and disgusting.” Kara grimaced.

 

“Of course, Love. I'll help you get downstairs.”

 

Getting Kara below deck was a much more massive undertaking than they had originally estimated. It took them ten minutes just to get Kara from the bed to the door, and another twenty-five to get her from the top of the stairs above deck down to the bottom. Lena had Kara strip down and sit on the stool before she began bathing her. In the back of her mind, she thought about the fact that Sam would have made fun of her until her face turned beet red for ever being this tender with anyone. Lena truly enjoyed the intimacy of moments like this where Kara was completely vulnerable and entirely comfortable with her. She’d never thought she would ever get to have something like this before she met Kara. To her, life had seemed like one big tragedy where she was the main character. Now, however, Lena got to experience light, love, and hope most importantly. For the first time in her life, even though they were in constant peril and surrounded by beings far more powerful than Lena had ever imagined, she felt hopeful for her future. Kara felt solid and sure under her hands despite her injuries, and Lena felt assured in her choice of partner. 

 

“Your hands feel nice. I just want to tell you that I really do enjoy every moment that I get to spend with you, and I admire you as a person. You’re really wonderful in every way and I think I’m privileged to call you mine.” Kara didn’t turn to face Lena, but instead spoke the words casually into the air in front of her as if they didn’t hold the weight of the world. Lena’s hands stilled and she felt tears well in the corners of her eyes. Kara’s sweet words curled around her heart tenderly and made her chest feel tight with overwhelming affection. She pressed a kiss to the top of Kara’s head. 

 

“I love you. So much.” Another kiss. “Let’s get you rinsed and get you back upstairs. I’ll make dinner for you and Onyx after we get you settled in. Maybe you can sketch for a while,” Lena suggested. 

 

“Mhm. I’m okay with going to bed pretty soon after dinner; I’m pretty exhausted.” Kara stretched, and a yawn left her mouth to accentuate her point. Lena quickly rinsed the soap from Kara’s body and helped her rinse her hair out before drying her off. She’d managed to keep Kara’s broken leg mostly dry, and she carefully toweled off the skin above the cast. 

 

“How bad is the pain?” She asked as she ran the towel up Kara’s side. 

 

“Pretty severe. I don’t know if I would be able to stay conscious right now if Astraeus hadn’t cast that spell. I feel like I’m being dramatic now, but I felt like I was dying. I could feel all the pieces crunching around while I moved.” Kara shuddered slightly, and Lena’s stomach churned uncomfortably at the thought of Kara being in so much pain. 

 

“I’m so sorry, darling,” Lena murmured with a frown. 

 

“It’s alright, Love,” Kara smiled at her, “I’m honestly so glad it was me that got caught instead of you.”

 

“While I agree the damage to my body would have been more severe,” Lena began before her voice began to tremble, “it still pains me so greatly to see you this way because I couldn’t help.”

 

“Hey, come here.” Kara patted her uninjured leg. Lena sat on Kara’s thigh and huddled closely to her chest. “It wasn’t your fault. Astraeus said that he purposefully put us at a disadvantage to test our adaptability.” 

 

“And I adapted by running away while you got hurt.” 

 

“What would have happened if you had stayed?” Kara asked genuinely. 

 

“I don’t know.” 

 

“ I think that I probably would have been so worried about you falling that I would have gotten myself hurt even worse. And then who knows? What if I fell and took us both down?” Kara speculated. Her words were gentle but honest, and some of Lena’s stress seemed to abate. 

 

“Okay. Agreed. But that doesn’t mean that I don’t wish I could have helped more,” Lena said. 

 

“Of course. I’m not asking you to not wish you could have helped, I’m only asking that you don’t feel guilty about what happened to me.” Kara punctuated her remark with a kiss to Lena’s cheek, and she hugged her closer to her chest. A smile broke Lena’s stoic demeanor as she soaked up Kara’s warmth and felt Kara’s magic reaching out to her. 

 

“Hey,” Kara pulled back and looked at Lena seriously, “I love you. You’re a powerful, capable woman, and I admire your strength and ability.” 

 

Lena’s smile quickly became watery as she held back tears of happiness. She pulled Kara into a kiss and thought about how lucky she was to love a woman like Kara. Even more unbelievable, Kara loved her back just as much and saw her for who she truly was. 

 

“Let’s go upstairs, Love.” Kara patted her back gently. Lena stood and helped Kara into a clean pair of clothes from the line they’d strung up the first time they’d washed laundry. Dinner was the same as lunch, and went by quickly. Lena found herself missing the fresh produce on Terra’s island, and longed to cook Kara a real meal. Lena quickly discovered how big Kara’s appetite was and came to the conclusion that the meager soup she’d made for Kara their first night together was not satisfactory. 

 

After dinner, Lena helped Kara painstakingly back up the stairs and into the cabin. Kara immediately collapsed on the bed as quickly as her leg would allow and made space for Lena at her side as she always did. Lena tucked herself into Kara’s side and found once again that her body fit perfectly against Kara’s as if they were made for each other. It amazed Lena that they could still find small moments of peace together like this. No matter how chaotic things became around them, Kara always made sure there was a calm place for Lena to rest her head at the end of the day. 

 

“I’m so tired. It’s not even late.” Kara heaved a heavy sigh and relaxed into the bed. 

 

“I know. Me too. I hope that we can finally rest for real when we get to Ion. I don’t think Astraeus would have helped us anymore than the one spell, so it will probably take a while for us to get there. He made it pretty clear that nothing about this matters to him.” Lena spoke softly as she succumbed to the warmth of Kara’s body, and slowly began to fade into sleep. 

 

“I hope the rest of the journey goes quickly. I’d like to feel solid ground under my body for more than one night,” Kara murmured. 

 

“Mm.. hmm…” Lena hummed softly as sleep overwhelmed her and the steady thrum of Kara’s heart soothed her worries. Kara felt the heaviness of sleep pulling at her eyelids as she struggled to stay awake long enough to join with Lena. Kara fell out of consciousness just a few moments too soon to join her and fell into a solitary, dreamless slumber. Kara’s sleep was heavy and felt like it was over in the blink of an eye. She opened her eyes to the same dust motes swirling through the early morning light that she had for the last month. The air was still and quiet aside from Lena’s soft breathing as she slumbered next to Kara. The sounds of the ocean that had surrounded her for weeks still floated softly to her ears.

 

Suddenly, the boat felt like it hit something, but not something like a monster or a massive wave. This felt more solid. This felt more steady. She waited for a moment. Waited for the boat to be rocked again, tossed side to side even, but it never came. The boat remained still. Kara focused and could faintly distinguish the sound of water lapping against the sides of their boat, and waves breaking over sand. Kara’s heart began to race and she tried to calm herself. 

 

“No way, no way, no way,” Kara thought to herself as she gently began to shake Lena awake. 

 

“Hm- what? What’s happening?” Lena asked groggily as Kara rocked her. 

 

“Lena, I think we’re here.” 

 

“What?” Lena sat straight up in bed and almost jostled Kara’s bad leg in her shock. 

 

“The boat stopped moving. I can hear a beach right outside. There are waves breaking. I think we’re here!” Kara spoke excitedly, her magic energy crackling and spilling into Lena eagerly. 

 

“Let’s go look!” Lena said and stood up quickly from the bed. She helped Kara carefully to her feet and made herself into a crutch as she had the day before. Onyx stood up and stretched to follow them as if he knew it was finally time to get off the boat as well. They made their way carefully outside and tried to remind themselves that this was all real. They tried to move carefully to spare Kara’s ankle, but it was difficult to contain their excitement. More than once, Kara almost fell over trying to hurry to the railing. 

 

Kara gasped and tried to comprehend everything in front of her as she stepped from the cabin out into the dim sunlight of the early morning. The orange glow kissed the black sand beach and reflected off of the countless glittering faces of each particle. Scattered randomly around the beach were green glowing masses of crystals the size of their boat jutting out of the sand. Their emerald glow faded as the sun crept slowly up over the horizon. The beach was small, more of an inlet than anything, and was capped on both ends by glittering cliffs of mottled grey and white stone. The cliffs were shorter than the ones on Kara’s beach back home, but Kara could see massive towering features more inland from where she stood that she never could have even dreamed of at home. Directly in front of them, the black sand beach eventually ran up into a grassy path that seemed to empty into the rest of the land. Kara couldn’t see much of the land close to them past the cliffs, but high in the sky, far inland from their location, Kara could see giant floating masses in the sky and part of an entire forest of trees so tall they disappeared into the clouds above them. 

 

As far as Kara could tell, the land seemed to build upward into a giant mountain hundreds of miles away from them, but she couldn’t get a good view from their low vantage on the beach. Only thanks to her super sense of sight could she barely make out small bare areas that ascended the mountain like stepping stones that seemed to be connected to each other by thin paths of dirt between the trees. Kara couldn’t imagine how large those paths and clearing had to be for her to be able to see them all the way from the shore, but they must have been massive. 

 

Onyx meowed loudly behind them, stretching deeply, and ran, leaping down off of the boat. As he sailed through the protective spell around their ship, his already large body melted and shifted until he was larger than any living creature that Kara had ever encountered on land. His tail alone was nearly eight feet long, slightly longer than his main body, and looked incredibly powerful, like a hundred pound whip. He turned his bright green eyes back up at them as if to say ‘ What? Aren’t you coming?’ and sat down with a huff in the sand. His long tail curled all the way around his feet and wrapped back around his front. 

 

“Hey Lena, what the hell just happened to your cat?!” Kara exclaimed as she stared over the railing at the massive cat sitting beneath them. Kara guessed that he had to be at least seven feet tall even when he was sitting. She almost couldn’t believe her eyes if not for all the strange things she’d seen in the past weeks. 

 

“I-I….” Lena spluttered and trailed off, mouth gaping in shock and confusion. Kara’s ears pricked up suddenly as she heard frantic, heavy footsteps running toward them. She squinted and could barely make out the shape of a giant hulking man running at an incredible speed toward the beach, directly towards their boat. He slowed slightly as he came closer, but only to a human sprint. The man running toward them was handsome, with what looked to be tattoos charting an entire half of his tan face. His chest was covered by a thin gray shirt that clung to his skin with water as fat little drops fell from his cropped black hair into his broad shoulders and darkened the fabric. He wore bright pink shorts that were clearly too small for him, looking as though they belonged to someone else. Kara didn’t know how he managed to fit his massive legs into those shorts without them tearing, but he managed. He came short of their boat and bent over to catch his breath. 

 

Onyx chirped at him, which now almost sounded like a bark rattling around in his barrel chest, and came to sit by his side. Kara’s confusion only increased when Onyx allowed the man to run an affectionate hand across his back. His chest heaved as he scratched Onyx behind his ear. The man looked up at Kara, and she could see that the tattoos on his face glittered around his eye, as if the lines held a void of stars in their ink. Nearly half of his face was blacked out by an intricate swirl of cosmic glitter and void with small geometric lines of plain skin peaking through. The eye that was surrounded in ink was pale and milky, as if it were blind, although it looked undamaged. His other eye shone a bright blue, bluer even than the sapphire Kara held in her pouch. 

 

“I’m so sorry I’m unprepared for your arrival, despite my part in its coordination.” The man's voice was deep, but soothing, and Kara thought briefly that it reminded her of her own father’s voice, but she knew that none of her memories from that early in life were in any sense reliable. His panting stopped and his breathing slowly returned to normal. “I did not expect Astraeus to send you along so early, else I would have been here to greet you upon your arrival. I was in the bathing quarters when Elyse told me of your arrival. I came as soon as I could. I- I do believe I grabbed some clothes that were not mine.” He seemed slightly embarrassed as he looked at his attire, and a shy smile crinkled the lines around his white eye. 

 

“Sorry- who are you and what in the world just happened to my cat?! ” Lena managed to ask rather exasperatedly. 

 

“My most sincere apologies.” He crossed his hand over his chest and bowed respectfully to them. “My name is Ion. Your cat is an old, old friend of mine. This is his original home, his true form as well.” 

 

“But… why?” Kara asked. “Why us? Why all of this? What’s going on here?” 

 

“I have many, many things to explain to the both of you, and many things to show you.” He saw Kara’s mangled leg for the first time as Kara shifted with a pained grimace on deck. His brow furrowed with something close to anger. “I didn’t know Astraeus would send you along injured, either. Come with me. I have food and drink aplenty for you to rest and rejuvenate. My friends and I can aid with your injury as well.” He smiled a bright white smile and Kara could sense sincerity in his voice and his demeanor. Kara could tell that Lena was just as disarmed by him. He was giant, like Kara, at least six and a half feet tall and completely muscle bound. He looked like he could pull a bear in half with his hands, but Kara sensed no ill will or danger from him. 

 

“At least answer me this: where are we exactly?” Lena asked, pleading. 

 

“In relation to your home? Directly on the other side of the planet. This is where all of the remaining magic creatures have made their home. The last magical place in the world. This is my home. Welcome to Vitalium.”
